#2834. Trump’s accounts traded up to $97.5 million in Nvidia as Huang gained White House access

President Donald Trump has had plenty of reason to cheer the rise of Nvidia.

The chipmaker helped propel the stock market that Trump is fond of touting. Its billionaire co-founder, Jensen Huang, became one of the president's favorite businessmen. And by the end of last year, Nvidia had become one of Trump's three largest individual stock holdings, worth between $12.5 million and $58 million, up from between $615,000 and $1.3 million a year earlier.

Since returning to the White House, Trump's investment accounts have bought or sold Nvidia shares at least 103 times — transactions worth a combined $27 million to $97.5 million, according to a new CNBC analysis of his annual and periodic financial disclosures, which were most recently updated through August.

By midday Monday, Trump's Nvidia stake, based on the value he reported holding in August, would be worth $4.5 million to $57.7 million with stock priced around $157. But the filings give broad dollar ranges for holdings rather than the number of shares, leaving his current stake unclear.

Now, Huang's resignation as Nvidia's CEO on Thursday, following revelations about his relationship with a woman suspected of spying for China and reports of affairs with company employees, has put those trades back in the spotlight.

Among them was a sale of $500,001 to $1 million on May 18, the first Monday after Trump returned from a state visit to China with Huang. Five days earlier, before Huang boarded Air Force One during a refueling stop in Alaska, Secretary of State Marco Rubio had briefed Trump on intelligence concerning Huang's relationship with the suspected spy, The New York Times reported.

Trump on Monday denied being briefed. Asked about the reported briefing from Rubio, Trump said, "I haven't heard that," adding that he did not know whether the woman, Alice Huang, was a Chinese spy.

The president's financial accounts also bought smaller amounts of Nvidia shares in the days immediately before and after the May 18 sale, part of a broader trading blitz. Such flurries have defined his second term: Trump disclosed more than 21,000 trades in 2025 alone, compared with roughly 500 during his entire first term, according to CNBC's analysis.

A White House spokesperson told CNBC independent advisors manage Trump's portfolio without his input. Trump Organization spokesperson Kimberly Benza said the arrangement is designed to "avoid even the appearance of a conflict of interest." The disclosures offer no evidence Trump directed the trades or that his advisors had access to classified intelligence.

Trump has long used the stock market as a scorecard for the economy, touting its record highs — sometimes when highs have not actually been reached — despite the turbulence caused by his tariffs and the Iran war.

"Look, we have the highest stock market we've ever had," Trump told ESPN commentator and political talk show host Stephen A. Smith on Thursday when pressed about Republicans' midterm losses.

Nvidia alone accounted for about 13.5% of the S&P 500's 2026 gains through September, according to an analysis by Choate Wealth that looked at how chip stocks have driven the year's performance.

Huang's rise in Trump's Washington has been nearly as striking as Nvidia's on Wall Street. The two have met or appeared together at least 15 times since Trump returned to office, according to a CNBC review of public records and news reports. Huang in June told The Associated Press that Trump even "calls me in the middle of the night."

At Trump's Jan. 20, 2025, inauguration, several Silicon Valley titans occupied prime seats, but Huang wasn't there. The two men had never met.

That changed on Jan. 31, 2025, when Huang visited the White House, capping a bruising week for Nvidia: Chinese AI startup DeepSeek had wiped $593 billion off the company's market value in a single day, according to LSEG data cited by Reuters.

Two days before the meeting, Trump's money managers bought between $1 million and $5 million in Nvidia stock.

For Huang, much of the relationship hinged on access to China. In April 2025, the administration effectively halted sales of Nvidia's H20 chips to China, prompting a $4.5 billion charge, according to an SEC filing. Huang argued that withholding U.S. chips would only accelerate China's development of its own.

The administration eventually relented. After another meeting with Trump in July 2025, Huang announced from Beijing that Nvidia expected to resume H20 sales. A week after Trump confirmed an August 2025 deal calling for 15% of the revenue from the company's chip sales to China to go to the U.S. government, his accounts also bought between $5 million and $25 million in Nvidia stock, one of several large tech investments that day.

Huang was also becoming a fixture of Trump's international diplomacy, joining him in Saudi Arabia and the U.K. Nvidia, already a $1 million donor to Trump's inaugural fund, would pledge another $25 million to his planned White House ballroom on condition of anonymity, The Washington Post first reported.

But behind the scenes, intelligence officials first briefed Trump last fall on Huang's relationship with the woman suspected of spying for China, The New York Times reported.

Trump nevertheless continued to ease restrictions on Nvidia's business in China, announcing Dec. 8 that the company could sell its more powerful H200 chips. His accounts had sold as much as $1 million in Nvidia stock four days earlier and bought more the day after.

In the first eight months of 2026, Trump's accounts made another 38 Nvidia trades worth a combined $9.4 million to $28.3 million, according to CNBC's analysis. A disclosure released Oct. 8 — the day Trump awarded Huang the National Medal of Science — showed purchases of up to $1.6 million and sales of up to $1 million in August alone.

Trump stood by Huang as Nvidia's board weighed his future last week, urging him to fight to keep his job, the Times reported.

With Huang out and Nvidia's shares tumbling, Trump described him Thursday as a "tremendous talent" who was "going through hell" — in the same interview in which he boasted of the market's record highs.

Trump is already floating a comeback for Huang, telling reporters Monday that reinstating him "certainly isn't a bad idea." But Nvidia's board is not considering his return, a person familiar with the matter told CNBC. The person asked not to be named because they were discussing internal matters."

Who says you can't tailgate an IPO?Traders are preparing for this week's blockbuster Anthropic IPO in different ways, including buying options on one publicly-traded fund that invests in private assets.The Destiny Tech100 (DXYZ) is a publicly-traded, closed-end fund that invests in equity, equity-linked instruments, or shares of private funds and venture companies to gain exposure to private companies. Currently, 14% of the fund's assets are tied to Anthropic, according to the company's website.Options volume on the fund jumped to more than 10x the 30-day average Monday, according to Cboe LiveVol data. Of the 42,300 contracts traded by midday, 41,800 were calls, with just 141 puts likely opened by buyers.

Just shy of $5.5 million in options premium on DXYZ traded on Monday, with almost all of it tied to calls, but just marginally more spent buying than selling calls, according to SpotGamma. #2831. Patriots players reveal how they plan to survive without Drake Maye

The Patriots' 2026 season went from mediocre to worse as Drake Maye reportedly suffered a significant lower leg injury in Munich on Sunday. The NFL Network reports that the Patriots quarterback could miss the rest of the season.

The Patriots returned home from Germany on early Monday morning, and now Maye is reportedly seeking more medical information about the injury. It's expected that Maye will at least miss an extended period of time.

Maye left Sunday's blowout loss after suffering the injury at the end of the Patriots' first offensive series. Following the game, MassLive spoke to several Patriots players about what the team needed to do to overcome the loss of their starting quarterback.

"First of all, it's our job. We got a job to do," defensive tackle Milton Williams said. "As long as I'm healthy and able, I'm gonna show up and try to do my part, try to be a leader and give this team and this organization everything I got because they believed in me and brought me here for a reason.

"It's tough right now, but that's how the league go, you know, go through trials and tribulations. I just got to step up and get ready to use this bye to get away, rest, regroup, and when we get back, get back to work."

The news on Monday was devastating for the Patriots. The No. 3 overall pick in 2024, Maye quickly became the franchise quarterback the organization was looking for after Tom Brady departed New England in 2020.

RELATED: Patriots top futures markets heading into bye week: Does the loss of Drake Maye tank the season?

Last season, Maye developed into one of the NFL's best quarterbacks, finishing as the league's MVP runner-up. Although this season hasn't been easy for the Patriots, Maye's shown the ability to take over games. Now, the Patriots will try to salvage this season without their best player. On Sunday, after Maye left the game, the offense managed just seven points.

Players say the team needs to band together.

"Just come together even closer," center Jared Wilson said. "Offense, defense, special teams, coaches, everyone just coming together as the family, (expletive) the team. Be a family. Hopefully that can kind of give us something."

"I think we got to have that tight-knit group and know that something's happening in the NFL," receiver Efton Chism III said. "Battling back from those injuries is huge. We have to try to get everyone on the same page."

"You just got to keep going. You keep fighting, keep punching," receiver Kyle Williams added. "At some point, we're going to break through that wall, you know what I'm saying? And when we break through that wall, everybody just got to stay on that side…. We got a bye week to reset."

Now, when the 5-5 Patriots return from their bye week, they'll do so with backup quarterback Tommy DeVito taking over. Rookie quarterback Behren Morton will now go from the No. 3 spot to the top backup spot.

DeVito came to New England last season and was the Patriots' No. 3 quarterback behind Joshua Dobbs. He was elevated to the team's top backup spot this year after the team released Dobbs.

DeVito played in nine games for the New York Giants in 2023. He finished that season with 1,101 yards with 8 touchdowns and 3 interceptions. In New York, DeVito had a 3-5 record as a starter.

Members of the Patriots offensive line say they need to do everything they can to help DeVito. Maye's injury on Sunday occurred when he was sacked by Aidan Hutchinson.

"Everyone obviously knows how much Drake means to this team," left tackle Will Campbell said. "And as (crappy) as it is, we have a lot of games left and hopefully at some point we can get (Drake) back. But for the time being, everyone in the locker room has faith in Tommy and he shows up to work every day and trust everybody around him to make the plays and just got to do everything we can to protect him and give him time to make plays."

#2830. Dan Campbell: I'm not telling players to stop celebrating because of Aidan Hutchinson's injury

Lions head coach Dan Campbell isn't going to tell his players to stop celebrating in the wake of defensive end Aidan Hutchinson's torn ACL.

Hutchinson will miss the rest of the season after injuring his right knee while celebrating a sack of Drake Maye in Sunday's game. Hutchinson jumped in the air and immediately grabbed his right knee after suffering the injury when he landed back on the turf in Munich.

During an appearance on 97.1 The Ticket on Tuesday, Campbell called the injury "one of those things that happened" and that "this game is built off of emotion" that he doesn't want to take away from his players.

"I hate it, I know he hates it. But I mean, the number of moves that I've seen Hutch make and the number of plyometrics, explosive movements, the spins, the one leg lateral movements, lateral balances, I'm just like, it was one of those things, man," Campbell said. "I hate it. I think we all understand what it is, and let's be smart, but no, I'm not gonna say, 'Nobody celebrates anymore.'"

Campbell recounted a story from his own playing days when he tore his biceps while celebrating a play and that didn't lead him to make celebration avoidance a rule for the Lions when he got the job. That doesn't make Hutchinson's injury any easier for the Lions to deal with, but it makes his response to questions about the advice he'll give in the future easier to understand.

#2829. Anthropic likely to price IPO above the current range of $70 to $78, sources tell CNBC

Anthropic is likely to price its IPO above the current range of $70 to $78, multiple sources told CNBC's David Faber on Tuesday.

The artificial intelligence company is planning to close the order books and lock in total demand at 4 p.m. ET on Tuesday, according to the people, who asked not to be named because they were not authorized to speak publicly. Anthropic is slated to list on the Nasdaq under the ticker symbol "ANT" and start trading on Nov. 19.

If Anthropic's IPO proceeds as planned, the company will debut roughly five months after SpaceX went public in a record offering that raised $85.7 billion. But Anthropic is facing a public market that's grown increasingly skeptical of the AI trade, as well as mounting and widespread antipathy toward the technology in the U.S.

A group of researchers, including Anthropic CEO Dario Amodei, co-founded the company in 2021 after defecting from OpenAI, now its chief rival. Anthropic is best known for developing a family of AI models called Claude, and it underwent a period of explosive growth in 2026, driven largely by the popularity of its AI coding tool, Claude Code.

For the nine months ended Sept. 30, Anthropic said it generated $33.24 billion in revenue, up roughly 1,200% from the same period last year, according to its prospectus. It reported a $104 billion net loss during the nine-month period, though most of that was due to the change in fair value of convertible notes and derivative liabilities. Anthropic lost $25.1 billion over the same stretch in 2025, the filing said.

Anthropic has long worked to establish a reputation as a safety-conscious AI company, and its prospectus included more than 90 pages of risk factors for investors to consider ahead of its listing.

"I think if we do this right, we can create an age of miracles where humanity is able to solve problems that have eluded us for millennia," Amodei said in Anthropic's roadshow video. "We have to make sure that all the risks, all the ways that things can go wrong with this technology, we very carefully address those risks as we bring the benefits and as we have a viable business at the same time."

"I invite you to join us on the next phase of this journey," Amodei said.

#2825. Trump has arrived in Asia. Here’s what he said about Iran and Deutsche Bank

U.S. President Donald Trump has arrived for his high-stakes trip to Asia, and took a range of questions on subjects from Iran to Deutsche Bank leaking details about his tax.

The president landed in Manila, Philippines, for the initial leg of his trip on Tuesday and answered rapid-fire questions from the press while meeting with other leaders.

Asked about a possible Iranian threat to Washington, D.C., he said that, if there were an attack, Iran would "pay a price the likes of which no country has ever paid before."

The question was about CNN reporting that U.S. intelligence suggested the country was planning to target senior members of Trump's administration with a dirty bomb or chemical weapon in the U.S. capital.

"We take it very seriously because they happen to be crazy," Trump said. CNBC has not independently verified CNN's report.

But the president also said he believed his administration was "very close to getting it done" when it came to the Iran war.

While Trump was airborne, the Financial Times reported Deutsche Bank had mistakenly leaked details of his taxes in March 2017, during his first presidential term. The mistake was kept from the White House at the time, per the FT.

Asked about this in Manila, the president said: "I paid a lot of tax."

"They shouldn't have done it. It's illegal for them to do it," he added.

Trump was also asked about Nvidia's announcement on Tuesday of a new National Security and Export Control Oversight Committee. The company, which reports earnings later on Tuesday, is reeling from the sudden departure of CEO Jensen Huang, who resigned last week after having an affair with a woman U.S. intelligence reportedly believes to be a Chinese spy.

"It's a great company. It's a great idea," Trump said.

Trump is due to fly to Shenzhen, China, where he is scheduled to meet Chinese President Xi Jinping and Russian President Vladimir Putin for a three-way meeting on Thursday, on the sidelines of the Asia-Pacific Economic Cooperation summit.

He is also due to hold a separate bilateral meeting with Xi. Asked by a reporter if he would have a separate meeting with only Putin, Trump said: "I don't know, but I would, if it's available, I would do that. He's coming. I mean, it's really with the guests. I am the guest of President Xi."

Trump met the leaders of Australia, India and Japan as part of the Quad grouping in the Philippines and the prime minister of Thailand. He is due to China on Wednesday.

Speaking at a dinner with Southeast Asian leaders as the event wrapped up for the day, Trump touted his record during his second term, citing investment into the U.S., trade deals made with countries represented by attendees at the dinner, energy infrastructure buildouts and America's AI industry.

Trump, who has led a campaign to rename artificial intelligence "super intelligence," or "SI," told leaders around the table that the U.S. is "leading the world" in the technology.

"We have the world's most powerful SI ecosystem," he said. "We're leading everybody by a lot, and we hope to keep it that way, and we will keep it that way."

He added that the U.S. has "the strongest and most capable military on the planet," pointing to investments in the armed forces and the country's defense sector.

"We make the greatest equipment. You saw that in Venezuela. You see it in Iran. You see it everywhere," Trump said.

#2824. AFC standings after Week 10: 4 tiers of teams, including the Browns in the 2nd tier

The AFC playoff standings will be finalized once the Baltimore Ravens face the winless Los Angeles Chargers on Monday Night Football, but the Cleveland Browns will exit Week 10 squarely in a playoff spot heading into their bye week. Are the Browns a great football team? Probably not, but they are young and winning football games despite their deficiencies, so that should be good enough in 2026.

Week 10's victory over the Houston Texans didn't lock up a playoff spot or eliminate the three-win visitors, but the result helped clarify the four different tiers of teams in the AFC after 10 weeks of the 2026 NFL season. These tiers are just an opinion and take into account a team's record, as well as their upside for the rest of the season, but not their NFL playoff hopes:

No one would be surprised if the Steelers or Ravens went on a positive run, but, in the same way, they, along with the Browns, have shown that they can play pretty poorly at times as well.

A big asterisk for the Patriots with QB Drake Maye injured in Week 10, with concern about how long he could be out. QB Tommy DeVito was not good at replacing Maye and does not have a strong history to fall back on.

Of the remaining teams with fewer than four wins, only the Texans made moves with this year in mind. Despite being 2.5 games out of the playoffs, anything can happen in the NFL, especially if they can get healthy, despite their current record.

The Ravens are hoping a little more time with new QB Jacoby Brissett will help them keep the ship afloat until Lamar Jackson is able to return, but Monday Night Football's loss to the previously winless Los Angeles Chargers was a poor start.

For the Raiders, can rookie QB Francisco Mendoza keep them in the playoff hunt, or did trading away Kirk Cousins signal the white flag being raised?

A lot of bad teams are losing a lot of games in this tier, with much closer to 0% chance of making the AFC playoffs than even a 10% chance, barring something wild.

How would you tier off the AFC, and where would you put the Cleveland Browns at this point?

#2823. Game Preview: Knicks at Raptors, November 17, 2026

No, it's not a typo or mix-up by your favorite sports blog on the worldwide web. The Knicks are, in fact, still in Toronto. New York's basketball team hopes the peameal bacon sandwiches and poutine go down well and that they can make it 15 straight wins against the reeling Raptors on Tuesday. Sunday showed how quickly a game like this can go south in the north. The Knickerbockers needed a charismatic late-game comeback to pull out an overtime thriller.

If Mike Brown and his men are looking for a less stressful evening, they should take a lesson from Sunday's near loss. Toronto controlled the glass for most of the game and got what felt like a gazillion offensive rebounds. Everyone from Collin Murray-Boyles and Scottie Barnes to Alijah Martin and Allen Graves found something underneath the Knicks' hoop. Some of that is to be expected against one of the best offensive rebounding teams in the league, but New York still can't let the Dinos stomp all over them on the glass.

Toronto is still without Kawhi Leonard, their big-time offseason Aspiration (sorry, acquisition). "Nobody did any, you know, wrongdoing," as he once said. He's just injured right now, and rumor has it he can't even make it to his newest marketing gigs. Without Leonard, expect the Raptors to lean harder into their strengths. They'll crash the offensive glass, force turnovers, and muck up the game, giving their superior athleticism, their emotions, and maybe even the crowd a chance to take over. Scotiabank Arena is a hard place to play, so the Knicks would do well to keep the Canucks quiet.

Scottie Barnes, in particular, needs to be silenced. He returned Sunday after missing four games with a groin strain and dumped a whopping 30 points on the Knickerbockers, almost willing his Dino friends to victory. His late-game scoring barrage was another sign that the always-talented 25-year-old might have taken another step forward. The ultra-athletic, rangy jack-of-all-trades once lacked the killer instinct and game of a true frontline scorer. This season, "Scottie B" is averaging 24.2 points per game. Apparently, he was really upset about how his club fumbled the game away on Sunday, so expect another big effort out of OG's old friend.

Barnes is a load to deal with, and New York has plenty of problems of its own. Mikal Bridges and Josh Hart are struggling mightily, leaving fans to wonder whether a change to the starting lineup could be in the cards. Landry Shamet is doing his best to fire up those discussions with one spirited effort off the pine after another, though it looks like Mike Brown will stick with his starters for now.

Pick-and-roll defense and protecting the paint have been issues from time to time, but New York has held up pretty well on that side of the ball. Only two teams in the league hold opponents to a lower field goal percentage than the Bockers, who also rank in the top four in defensive rating. On offense, they're barely in the top half of the league, and shooting is a major reason why. The five worst teams by field goal percentage are Brooklyn (3-9), Utah (1-13), Charlotte (4-9), Milwaukee (1-13), and your very own Knicks (7-6). Yup, it's almost a miracle they're above .500 given how poorly they've shot.

Other construction sites include size on the wing, OG Anunoby's early-season inconsistency, and the backup center position. Andre Drummond played himself off the floor Sunday night, looking stuck in molasses against the pick-and-roll. James Wiseman saw meaningful minutes for only the second time this year. He held up decently for roughly 180 seconds, but Mike Brown still has plenty to worry about at backup center. It'll be interesting to see how he plays it Tuesday night.

Same goes for the Knicks in general. Facing the same team twice in a row is weird, especially in the same building, even when you've won 14 straight against them. On paper, New York matches up well with Toronto. The Raps don't like to switch on defense, they put a traditional center on Towns, and they don't have the shooters to punish the Knicks for packing the paint. OG is always highly motivated against his former club, and we all know what that means for opponents. After spending the first six and a half seasons of his NBA career north of the border, the former Raptor surely had a few restaurant tips up his sleeve Monday evening. For a second dessert, we'd find win No. 15 against Toronto quite tasty, however it comes about.

#2822. Will Rams keep using two kickers?

The Los Angeles Rams have been using Brandon McManus for field goals and Josh Karty for kickoffs, and how much longer will Sean McVay keep doing this? The Rams have the bye week to make changes, or keep rolling with the two kickers. Using two kickers is uncommon, but whatever works.

McManus has been perfect on field goals and extra points since joining the Rams, while Karty has been solid when his "dirty kicks" work, but Karty has also had some penalties on kickoffs giving opponents excellent starting position. Karty has been inconsistent throughout his career.

It's been interesting to see the Rams use two kickers, but is it something they'll keep doing or will the experiment be shutdown after the bye week? It's a storyline worth watching.

"After losing Blake Grupe to injury, the Rams didn't stop by adding Brandon McManus, their third kicker this season. They continued by re-signing Joshua Karty. Karty was released last season when his 'knuckle kickoff' attempts tarnished his field-goal accuracy.His return was meant to optimize LA's defensive field position on kickoffs, with McManus used exclusively for field goals and extra points. Unfortunately, the results of Karty's kickoffs over three games are less than encouraging.

The risk was worth taking. Deploying Karty exclusively for his touchback-inducing knuckleball, thus spotting opposing offenses at the 20-yard line, is certainly innovative thinking"

"Even their 6-4 record suggests they're a good team, even if they're no longer the on-paper superteam they were once heralded as in September. The Rams enter their Week 11 bye tied for the same record as the Packers and Seahawks, who are seventh and eighth in the NFC.

Through 10 games in 2023, they were 4-6 and still made the playoffs. In 2024, they were 5-5 and once again made the postseason. So technically, they're in better shape now than they were in those two seasons."

"A lot of people were writing off the Los Angeles Rams after their 2-4 start. There's no question it wasn't the way they hoped to open the year after being the Super Bowl favorites, but just as they did in 2023 and 2024, the Rams have climbed out of that early hole and are right back in the playoff hunt.

Thanks to a four-game winning streak, Los Angeles is now 6-4 and entering a much-needed bye before the toughest part of the schedule hits. As bleak as things looked a month ago, the Rams actually have the same record as the team currently holding onto the No. 7 seed in the NFC, and that's not going to change in Week 11, either."

#2821. Homebuilder sentiment plunges to 14-year low, leading more builders to drop prices

Sentiment among U.S. homebuilders dropped to the lowest level in 14 years, as higher mortgage rates, higher costs for energy, materials and labor, as well as weaker consumer confidence, led to more builders cutting prices.

Builder confidence in the single-family housing market fell four points to 25, according to the National Association of Home Builders/Wells Fargo Housing Market Index, or HMI. That is the lowest since April 2012, when the market was just recovering from the housing crash and foreclosure crisis. Last November, the index stood at 38.

Mortgage rates pulled back slightly in October, but surged higher again last week. The average rate on the 30-year fixed hit 7.51% on Monday, according to Mortgage News Daily. Last year at this time it was 6.38%.

Of the HMI's three components, the index measuring current sales conditions fell six points to 28, and future sales expectations dropped two points to 30. Buyer traffic declined two points to 16 — its lowest level in 15 years, excluding the start of the pandemic in 2020.

"Builders are confronting multiple challenges in today's economy," Bill Owens, NAHB chairman and a home builder and remodeler from Worthington, Ohio, said in a release. "Stepped-up immigration enforcement is keeping legal workers off job sites, deepening labor shortages and driving up costs. Higher fuel prices are prompting subcontractors to raise their rates sharply, and with fewer buyers in the market, more builders are scaling back construction."

More builders are now cutting prices as they try to get buyers in the door. In November, 46% of them reduced prices, the highest share in the post-Covid period, according to the report. The share of builders using sales incentives also hit a post-Covid high of 68%. That was the 20th consecutive month in which at least 60% of builders used them.

"The housing market faces a range of supply-side challenges, including building material costs pushed higher by tariffs, an ongoing shortage of construction workers and excessive regulations that undermine housing affordability," Robert Dietz, NAHB's chief economist, said in the release. "Mortgage rates above 7%, softer consumer confidence and an uncertain economic outlook are holding buyers back."

In the three-month moving averages for regional HMI scores, the Northeast dropped four points to 34, the Midwest fell five points to 35, the South fell three points to 27, and the West posted a three-point decline to 23.

#2820. Game Preview: San Antonio Spurs vs Detroit Pistons

I know that (as my colleague Charlie Thaddeus pointed out) there's a tendency to embellish in the basketball blogosphere and on social media, but I feel like the significance of this win streak has really snuck up on us all.

And I get it. It's a weak portion of the schedule, no doubt about it. The Spurs have only played four teams currently at .500 or better during this run, with the benefit of some injury absences.

And yes, it's very early in the season, as opposed to when everyone else in rhythm and fighting for seeding and playoff/play-in berths. The competitive desperation just isn't on the same level.

Still, that's five out of twelve games, and you can really only play the teams in front of you. That's just how it is.

Consider for a moment that with a win against the Pistons, this win streak would tie for 4th longest in franchise history.

Do you know how many times the Spurs won 13+ games in the Duncan era? Just five times.

How about the David Robinson era? Just four.

[Side note: How crazy is it that the combination of Duncan and Robinson only resulted in one 13+ game win streak?!]

Consider that if the Spurs win this game, their next three games will be against three of the bottom four teams in the Western Conference (The Clippers, The Pelicans, and The Kings).

They will have a very good shot at extending this streak to 16 games, which would be the 3rd longest streak in Spurs team history.

Tim and David only managed to win 16+ consecutive games once each.*

(*In the regular season. Duncan did it twice if you count the combined 20-game win streak in 2012 from April 12th to the end of Game 2 of the Western Conference Finals. Personally, I try to forget that one.)

Consider that the Spurs had two(!) separate 11-game win streaks last season, and went undefeated over the month of February, and still couldn't get to 12, much less 13.

Consider that they've done this without the debut of an important off-season addition in Tobias Harris, and with short absences from both De'Aaron Fox and Devin Vassell.

It might not feel like it, but all things considered, this streak is a pretty big deal.

The Pistons could give them real trouble, though.

While they don't have as explosive an offense as the Spurs (who are 4th in points, 3rd in Offensive Rating vs. 12th and 13th), the Pistons rebound well enough (13th), pass well enough (10th in assists, 9th in assist percentage), and both avoid turnovers (7th in turnover percentage and assist-to-turnover ration) and force them (5th in steals, 2nd in blocks) to such an extent that they really have no glaring weaknesses to exploit.

The Spurs will have to play patient, tough, mistake free basketball against them more than most.

Even the Pistons' three-point shooting can take advantage of San Antonio sloppiness, as they sit in the top 10 in three-point percentage (9th), and their Defensive Rating is only a few ticks behind San Antonio's (6th vs. 3rd).

Yes, the Spurs will have to play exceptional ball to put Detroit away. The good news is that they have been.

Over the last 12 games the Spurs are 2nd in points scored, 1st in +/-, 1st in limiting fouls, 1st in limiting turnovers (shocking, I know), 1st in rebounds, 1st in blocks, 1st in offensive rating, 1st in net rating, 1st in player impact estimate, 2nd in 2nd chance points, 2nd in fast-break points, 1st in points in the paint, 1st in limiting points off of turnovers, and 5th in field-goal percentage. *whew*

And all of that in spite of being 23rd in three-pointers made, 15th in three-point percentage, 13th in effective field goal percentage, and 13th in free-throw attempt rate.

Basically, you have to pray that this team has a cold shooting night.

And (as the Mavericks recently learned) that still might not be enough. God forbid they starting hitting their open looks (they're near the bottom of the league in actual shooting vs quality of looks).

I know they made the Finals last season, but I just cannot emphasize how special this Spurs team really is.

If they get even a little hot from outside, my money's on them.

But my money would be on them anyway. They're just that good.

Spurs Injuries: Tobias Harris: Out (Calf), Jayden Quaintance: Out (Knee)

Pistons Injuries: Jalen Duren: Out (Hamstring), Duncan Robinson: Out (Quad), Isaiah Joe: Doubtful (Hip)

#2816. Nvidia has lost $2 trillion in value. Here's what options traders expect from earnings

Jensen Huang's forced exit from Nvidia shocked Wall Street and Washington, wiped $2 trillion off its market value, and raised serious questions about national security and regulation of the AI complex. Traders, for the moment, must content themselves with the comparatively mundane business of Tuesday's earnings, and options traders are expecting big moves.

With volatility in the stock near the highest since April 2025, options traders are now pricing a 7% reaction in the stock after its report, the biggest implied move going into an Nvidia report since February 2025, and well above the median move of 3.2% over the last eight quarters.

Implied volatility reached 68 Monday, compared to under 40 in early October, according to ThinkOrSwim data. Nvidia vol reached a multi-year high above 92 in the aftermath of President Trump's tariff Liberation day in April '25. In other words, the company that for much of the AI boom was the most reliable winner in the market, is now more volatile than all but 35 companies in the S&P 500.

"Longer term, I see significant downside risk unless Nvidia can attract an exceptional leader, someone like Tim Cook or AMD's Lisa Su," Nigam Arora, founder of the Arora report, said in a text ahead of the company's after-market report on Tuesday.

Since hitting all-time highs in October, Nvidia shares have fallen 35%, shedding $2 trillion in market capitalization along the way. Including Nvidia, there are only five companies in the S&P 500 worth more than $2 trillion. However, there is evidence that bulls have been holding out hope while bears snatch up profits: the ratio of open puts to call contracts has actually widened towards calls, according to Barchart data. The ratio is currently 0.87, down from a one-year high of 1.02 two weeks ago and the lowest since the first week of October.

The most popular contract by volume on Monday was the 155-strike put expiring at the bell Monday, but the most popular trade for later expiry was the 160-strike call expiring Thursday, and the second-most-common purchase was the 190-strike call expiring Friday, SpotGamma data show.

One catch is that most call contracts traded Monday looked like neutral to bearish positions opened by traders selling calls. Of the 5 million options traded on Monday, 3 million were calls, but more were likely opened by sellers than buyers. Of the $2.3 billion in options premium traded on Nvidia Monday, $1.2 billion was tied to puts, SpotGamma data show.

With bears taking profits in puts the past month, there's another potential snag on open interest. There are very few open put contracts at or below the $157 level at which the stock closed Monday. That suggests any bad surprise would find many traders with little downside protection.

By the same token, there's notable open interest in call contracts from the 175-strike and above, suggesting profit-taking on a bounce could limit Nvidia's recovery past levels it breached over the past week.

Either way, Tuesday's earnings could provide fireworks.

#2811. Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double

Observations after Sixers beat Bucks to move to 3-0, Embiid posts triple-double originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers stayed unbeaten Saturday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena, earning a 127-116 win over the Bucks and improving to 3-0 on the season.

Joel Embiid had his 10th career regular-season triple-double, tallying 28 points, 11 rebounds and 10 assists.

On his 30th birthday, Jaylen Brown posted 27 points and five assists. Tyrese Maxey had 26 points. VJ Edgecombe scored 17 and grabbed 13 boards.

The Sixers' absences on the first night of a back-to-back included LeBron James (low back/sciatica management). Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said pregame that he did not know yet who would be available for his team's matchup with the Pistons on Sunday.

Here are observations on the Sixers' victory Saturday:

Dean Wade made his Sixers debut and started in James' place. He'd missed the first two games of the regular season with a left ankle sprain.

The 3-and-D forward scored the night's first points by a sinking a corner three-pointer on the opening possession.

Embiid also had a sharp start. He moved well, made good decisions and served as the main hub of his team's offense in the early going.

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center has often enjoyed facing Myles Turner. He entered the night with an average of 29.4 points in 18 previous games against the Bucks big man.

Embiid did a little bit of everything in the first quarter, posting eight points, four assists and four rebounds over nine minutes. After snatching an offensive board, Embiid drained a fadeaway jumper to give the Sixers a 10-1 lead.

One of Embiid's assists was quite stylish. He found Edgecombe open beyond the arc with a behind-the-back bounce pass. Edgecombe knocked down the shot and wound up going 3 for 4 from three-point range in the first quarter.

The Sixers added rookie Labaron Philon Jr. to their rotation. Jabari Walker stayed in the mix, though he didn't enter until the 7:36 mark of the second quarter.

Philon played four minutes in his NBA debut and missed his lone shot attempt, a corner three. On defense, Philon allowed a couple of blow-bys off the dribble. He generally looked like he belonged on offense, albeit without any production in his brief run.

Maxey banked in a half-court heave at the first-quarter buzzer to put the Sixers up nine points. The Sixers couldn't carry forward any momentum, however, and began the second quarter poorly.

Adem Bona had another rough stint amid a subpar start to the season. After Anfernee Simons missed a transition layup try, Ryan Rollins' three lifted the Bucks into a 40-37 lead.

The dreadful stretch for the Sixers on both sides of the ball called to mind how they'd fallen into a 29-point deficit Thursday night vs. the Cavaliers before clawing out a comeback win.

The Sixers mustered just seven points in first six minutes of the second quarter and had many stagnant possessions that concluded with contested jumpers. The team's transition defense was dismal for much of the second period. Milwaukee had the game's first 19 fast-break points.

The Sixers regained the lead late in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope made two three-balls in the second and nabbed three first-half steals. Embiid assisted a go-ahead, and-one Walker layup.

Brown hit a three with 11.8 seconds left in the second quarter. Unsurprisingly, his variety of shotmaking skills were useful without James in the lineup.

Even when James or Embiid sit, the Sixers should have a simple advantage in terms of volume of stars over most opponents this year. None of Brown, Embiid or Maxey had the sort of off night that would have improved the Bucks' odds of a road win. They'd all passed the 20-point mark by the end of the third quarter.

Maxey notched another final-possession bucket, this time with a more conventional make. He canned a jumper from the right elbow with 1.7 seconds left in the third to extend the Sixers' lead to 97-87.

The Bucks made a late push in the fourth quarter. Kel'el Ware's put-back, and-one layup cut the Milwaukee's deficit to 117-114.

The Sixers ultimately handled business. Embiid reached his triple-double by assisting a second-chance Brown jumper and and Caldwell-Pope soon drilled a dagger corner three. Embiid even threw down a last-minute dunk for good measure.

For the second consecutive season, the Sixers have a 3-0 start. They got to four wins last year before dropping their first game.

#2808. Week 11 power rankings: Rams inch closer to top-10 heading into bye

The Los Angeles Rams are riding into their bye week on a four-game winning streak. Granted, those wins were against some lowly teams with a gauntlet of games in the second half of the year, but the Rams now have a week to assess their roster and make plans for a playoff push.

L.A.'s biggest issue is their health. Davante Adams and Terrence Ferguson might return after the bye week, but Puka Nacua, Coleman Shelton and Steve Avila probably won't. While these absences didn't tank the Rams in Week 10 against the Arizona Cardinals, not having them for the hardest stretch of their schedule could be detrimental.

Even still, experts moved the Rams up slightly as a borderline top-10 team. Most had the Rams between Nos. 11 and 12, while Yahoo Sports and Sports Illustrated both ranked L.A. No. 10.

Here is a roundup of the NFL power rankings heading into Week 11.

Who knew that fifth-year RB Ronnie Rivers was their closer? The Rams have certainly been disappointing to date, but give them credit for winning games they need a bit unconventionally.

With all the injuries the Rams have dealt with on offense, they've had to lean on their running back tandem of Corum and Kyren Williams. On Sunday, Corum ran for 100 yards and a touchdown on 17 carries and finished with a career-high 20 touches. It was his sixth straight game with a touchdown. Corum has been a key part of the Rams' rushing success during a four-game winning streak in which they've outrushed their opponents 653-175 (plus-478). According to ESPN Research, that is the team's largest rushing yards differential over a four-game span since 1984, the year Hall of Famer Eric Dickerson ran for an NFL-record 2,105 yards.

Don't look now, but the supposed Dream Team has won four straight to salvage some respectability out of their season and maybe, just maybe, turn them into a threat again. The way the Rams are hemorrhaging players, it might be too little, too late. They will be without their two best wideouts, Davante Adams and Puka Nacua, for a while, and they've got the Packers, Chiefs, 49ers, Cowboys and Seahawks lined up when they come out of their bye week. Never mind what they did to the Cardinals in Week 10. They're just not an offensive juggernaut anymore. They'll need their defense to come up big if they want to stay in the playoff chase.

They dominated the Cardinals without Puka Nacua, which is a big deal. The run game came alive, which they will need.

The Los Angeles Rams won their first game since placing Puka Nacua on injured reserve with a knee injury. They did so in a convincing fashion and showed that they have enough offensive talent to field a functional offense while Nacua and Davante Adams are sidelined.

However, there's a big difference between having a capable offense and being a top-tier Super Bowl threat. Los Angeles could still be the latter, and it is on a four-game winning streak, but it is about to face a higher degree of difficulty.

After their Week 11 bye, the Rams will face a brutal stretch that includes games against the Green Bay Packers, Kansas City Chiefs, San Francisco 49ers, Dallas Cowboys, and Seattle Seahawks (twice).

The Rams have won four in a row. They're still more likely than not to miss the playoffs — 44.2% chance to make it, via DVOA — because they have by far the NFL's toughest remaining schedule. But the Rams played well, albeit against a bad Cardinals team using rookie quarterback Carson Beck in his first career start. The Rams won 34-6 by running it 46 times for 202 yards. Konata Mumpfield provided some hope at receiver with Puka Nacua and Davante Adams out, with three catches for 81 yards. L.A.'s defense limited Arizona to six first downs.

It's still a hard path to the playoffs because of the schedule and injuries, but the Rams are hanging around.

The Rams pivoted from 13 personnel to 20 personnel (two running backs, no tight ends). As I wrote in the offseason, it's not about following Sean McVay and doing what he does. It's about doing what no one else is prepared to stop.

#2806. James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays ‘before they actually happen'

James reaches assists milestone, keeps seeing plays 'before they actually happen' originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Tyrese Maxey admitted that he wasn't entirely ready for LeBron James' first pass of the afternoon Sunday.

Maxey cut through the paint and James' bounce pass was perfect, but the Sixers' All-NBA guard missed his layup. He's not fully accustomed yet to James' precognitive gifts.

"There's still some times when you're surprised," Maxey said after the Sixers' win over the Pacers. "… I was cutting through just to cut and create space. The ball hit me in the chest and I missed the layup. I was like, 'Ah shoot, my bad.' He was like, 'Yeah, you weren't looking.'"

James has been trying to teach his teammates that he'll spot openings they may not notice.

"I just tell my guys, 'Always pay attention to me when I've got the ball,' because I definitely see plays develop before they happen," James said.

For the most part, James' teammates adhered to his request on the latest historic day in a milestone-filled career. By setting up VJ Edgecombe's layup with 1:19 left in the fourth quarter, James moved into third place on the NBA's all-time assists list, passing Jason Kidd.

The 22-time All-Star recorded 11 assists, his most so far as a Sixer, and now sits at 12,092 in his career. Chris Paul (12,552 assists) and John Stockton (15,806) are the only two players above James.

"It's obviously super humbling," James said. "It's something that I didn't foresee in my career, but I knew assists meant a lot to me. Just the movement of the ball, being able to find my teammates, putting the ball on time and on target.

"And also just being linked with some of the greats that have ever played this game and a dear friend of mine, J-Kidd. It's really special."

James is at or near the top of many all-time leaderboards. He's first in NBA history in points, games and minutes, sixth in steals and fifth in triple-doubles.

Passing has always been a core element of his game.

"First of all, he's got the vision and the awareness," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He's got the size to see. I think he's got some anticipation. Just a half-second before a play happens, he knows how the defense is moving. … And he throws crisp passes. Some of those are cooking out to the corner three guys, so it doesn't let the close-out get there nearly as quickly."

Whether it's a long-range rocket, a drive-and-kick dish or a cross-court feed from the post that suggests he might have eyes in the back of his head, James has always enjoyed finding a wide range of ways to help his teammates.

"For someone that talented, he's very unselfish," Maxey said. "He really prides himself on getting guys open."

As James recalled Sunday, he had exceptional passing tools long before he entered the NBA 24 seasons ago.

"Since I was a kid, I've had the ability to see plays before they actually happen," he said. "Seeing movement, seeing cracks in the defense before they were even open … it's something that's been with me since I was a kid.

"I just had that vision and it just stuck with me throughout my playing days."

#2803. NBA Head Coach Calls LeBron James an “AI Machine”

Before Sunday's matchup between the Philadelphia Sixers and Indiana Pacers, Pacers head coach Rick Carlisle was asked about LeBron James and said, "… he knows all the plays.. He's a walking NBA basketball AI machine"

James has played the most games in NBA history and has faced or played alongside nearly 40% of all players. The four-time NBA champion has seen it all, and with LeBron's memory and basketball IQ, regarded as one of, if not the, best of all time, it is no surprise that any play the Pacers could run, James can read and stop.

On Sunday afternoon, James helped the Sixers defeat the Pacers 131-124, improving to 11 -3. Still, the main storyline wasn't the win; it was LeBron, who dropped 15 points, grabbed seven rebounds, and dished out 11 assists. With his 11th assist, James surpassed Jason Kidd for third all-time.

To catch second-place Chris Paul, LeBron needs 460 assists, which is very doable. Currently, the 41-year-old averages seven assists per game, and at that pace it will take a little less than 70 games, which would mean LeBron would climb past Paul around this time next season.

If anyone thinks James can get first, the NBA's all-time leading scorer would have to play six and a half full seasons at the current pace of seven assists per game, which would put James in year 30 and a 48-year-old man. Safe to say the record set by John Stockton won't be touched anytime soon.

Through 14 games, the biggest impact LeBron has had on this Sixers squad is on the defensive end. Philly is currently seventh with a defensive rating of 110.3, a major improvement over last season, when the Sixers were 17th.

James acts as an on-court coach, calling out the opposing team's offensive sets and plays, which allows the Sixers' key perimeter players like Jaylen Brown, Tyrese Maxey, and VJ Edgecomb to be more confident in gambling and, in turn, has led to just over 8 steals per game for the Sixers.

Philadelphia 76ers forward LeBron James (23) looks on against the Indiana Pacers during the fourth quarter at Xfinity Mobile Arena. Photo Credit: Bill Streicher, Imagn Images via Reuters Connect

Most NBA players, as they approach their mid-to-late 30s, lose their athleticism, often regress, and eventually retire. That's why people say Father Time also wins. But James is still playing at a high level at age 41 despite his athleticism being nowhere near what it was a decade ago.

The reason James is still great is a testament to the generational basketball IQ the 22-time All-Star possesses, always being in the right place at the right time and making plays that very few humans can anticipate. It is said that no human can defeat Father Time, but maybe an AI machine can.

James is the fourth option on this Sixers team, which is totally different from the previous 22 years when James was either the first or second option. This is a different version of him than in years past, but the rest of the team has absorbed the knowledge and leadership. Maxey is an early-season MVP candidate, and Shaquille O'Neal called the Sixers the best team in the NBA. There are still some problems, but if the Sixers hoist the Larry O'Brien trophy, a large part of that will be because of LeBron's AI-like knowledge.

#2800. Andy Reid defends Xavier Worthy, who had no catches in 57 snaps

Chiefs quarterback Patrick Mahomes completed 22 passes to eight different receivers and threw five touchdowns in Sunday’s 35-13 win over the Falcons. None of his completions went to Xavier Worthy.

Worthy has 33 catches for 278 yards and a touchdown in nine games this season. The Chiefs have not used him in the run game.

On Sunday, he led all Kansas City wide receivers with 57 snaps and drew three targets. He also made a special teams mistake, allowing the Falcons to recover the first successful onside kick of the season in the NFL.

Coach Andy Reid was asked about Worthy’s lack of production, which had to be at least part of the reason the Chiefs traded for Josh Downs last week. Downs had four catches for 53 yards and a touchdown in his Kansas City debut.

“He just needs to keep doing his thing and working, and he’ll be fine,” Reid said Monday, via Charles Goldman of AtoZ Sports. “Some days will be his, and some days it goes to other guys. I mean, that’s just how this thing works. We’re spreading it around. Pat loves spreading it around and does a great job with it. Our coaches love doing that. It’s tough on defenses. Everybody’s kind of getting a little piece of the pie. You saw when he was only the guy against the Chargers and his production there was great. So, it’s not that he’s not capable of that.”

Reid further explained that the Falcons’ game plan was to take Worthy out of the game, which “allowed other guys to be available.”

Worthy, a first-round pick in 2024, had his best season as a rookie when he made 59 catches for 638 yards and six touchdowns, while also running for 104 yards and three touchdowns on 20 carries.

In the 23 games he’s played since, Worthy has 75 catches for 810 yards and two touchdowns, with 11 carries for 87 yards and no touchdowns.

#2796. Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return

Paul George flips the script — and the bird — in Philadelphia return originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

PG-13 was rated R in his return to Philadelphia.

The middle finger Paul George directed toward one baseline fan only reinforced that rating.

Tuesday night was expected to revolve around Jaylen Brown and Jayson Tatum sharing an NBA court as opponents for the first time. Instead, the other headliner in the summer's blockbuster trade stole the show.

George scored a game-high 36 points in Boston's 123-101 win over the Sixers. He shot 14 for 22 from the floor, made six of his 11 three-point attempts and scored 14 consecutive Boston points in the fourth quarter.

The game was tied at 79 with 3:19 remaining in the third quarter. Boston outscored Philadelphia 44-22 from that point forward.

The Celtics went nine minutes and 20 seconds without making a two-point field goal during the second half. Instead, they made nine consecutive field goals from three, the second-longest such streak by a Sixers opponent over the last 30 seasons.

Payton Pritchard opened the fourth with consecutive threes. George followed with eight straight points, helping the Celtics begin the quarter on a 14-0 run that turned a competitive game into a rout.

George's return carried a complicated history. His two seasons with the Sixers were repeatedly interrupted by injuries, but he also played a significant role in their most memorable playoff run in years.

Last spring, George helped the Sixers overcome a 3-1 deficit to eliminate Boston in seven games. He averaged 17.4 points and shot 55 percent from three during the series.

Tyrese Maxey was happy to see his former teammate Tuesday, even if he disliked what followed.

"It was good to see Paul," Maxey said. "I love him, love his family. … He gave us everything he had while he was here. We know what he did in the playoffs last year. Great person, great family, and we'll see him again."

Maxey then added one important qualifier.

George instead scored from nearly everywhere.

"He had some open threes. He had some pull-up threes. He had some middies," Maxey said. "He played within the game, too. That's what Paul does. He plays within the game. He takes what the defense gives him, and we gave him too many open looks tonight.

"PG, we know what PG does. We shouldn't even be allowing him to get up 11 threes, and that's on us."

Nick Nurse watched George attack the Sixers in several ways.

"He really had it going," Nurse said. "He'd post up, and-one, fallaways. He went down the lane, high archer off the glass, and-one. He scored in about every way he could tonight."

George heard boos during his return and said the arena carried the atmosphere, though not quite the intensity, of a playoff game.

"This building can be very electric as well," George said. "It was fun. It was fun to play in this environment."

One interaction extended beyond the usual reception for a returning player.

During a stoppage, the ball rolled toward a fan seated along the baseline. George said the fan looked directly at him before dropping the ball instead of returning it.

"There was a fan being an a–," George said. "The ball rolled to him. He looked me dead in my eyes, dropped the ball on the ground as if I'm a dog to go fetch or go get it, instead of passing the ball to me like a normal fan would do.

"And so, yeah, then I rolled him a middle finger in return."

#2795. Bryson DeChambeau Makes Major Career Move Amid PGA Tour Ban Fears

Bryson DeChambeau faces an uncertain period in his professional golf career after LIV Golf's collapse. The two-time U.S. Open champion is among the biggest names affected by the league's shutdown, raising questions about where he will compete regularly in 2027.

The American's situation is complicated by the PGA Tour's rules regarding former LIV players. A potential year-long suspension could keep him away from the American circuit until August 2027, making other competitive opportunities important as he considers his next steps.

DeChambeau remains scheduled to compete at the Nedbank Golf Challenge in South Africa in December.

James Corrigan of "The Telegraph" reported that Bryson DeChambeau submitted his application before Sunday's deadline and has secured Category 1 membership for the 2027 DP World Tour season.

The two-time major champion will receive guaranteed entry into every event through the membership category. The reported move follows LIV Golf's collapse after its proposed relaunch failed to secure the investment it needed from BC Partners.

The league now faces liquidation, leaving players to find alternative opportunities to keep competing at the highest level.

DeChambeau's 2024 U.S. Open victory made him eligible for the DP World Tour's Category 1 membership. A strong season on the European circuit could also offer a route back to PGA Tour status.

Finishing inside the top 10 on the Race to Dubai money list could earn him a PGA Tour card for 2028, subject to the applicable eligibility rules.

However, his reported membership does not mean an immediate return to the American circuit.

DeChambeau is expected to face a 12-month suspension following his final LIV appearance, potentially keeping him out of PGA Tour events until August 2027.

His immediate schedule includes the Nedbank Golf Challenge from December 3-6 in Sun City, South Africa.

The Hero Dubai Desert Classic in January is also expected to feature prominently in his plans, although that appearance has not been confirmed in the supplied information. DeChambeau recently said he would approach his next chapter with "humility and respect for the people who carried this game long before me."

McIlroy also welcomed the prospect of his involvement, saying DeChambeau "brings eyeballs" to tournaments.

While "The Telegraph" reported that he submitted his application, Reuters noted that his application status had not been publicly confirmed.

Beyond his playing future, DeChambeau is also beginning a new project away from professional competition.

The American is partnering with Cook Land Company to develop his first golf course near Clovis, California, where he grew up.

The proposed 450-acre development, called The Orchard Golf Club, will include an 18-hole championship course, practice facilities, housing and a farmers' market.

DeChambeau's new architecture venture, DeChambeau Designs, will develop the project.

The course is expected to reflect the property's agricultural history, with a different variety of fruit or nut tree planned for each hole.

DeChambeau also intends to introduce programs that expand access to golf for local children and schools.

However, the project still faces regulatory hurdles.

Clovis economic development director Chad McCollum told California public radio station KVPR that no development applications had been submitted as of Friday.

DeChambeau hopes to secure the necessary approvals within 18 months.

The project gives DeChambeau another way to contribute to golf while his competitive future remains uncertain. His 2027 schedule and potential return to the PGA Tour will remain important questions as the sport adjusts to LIV Golf's collapse.

#2785. Ravens’ top-five draft odds now exceed their fading playoff chances

The Ravens have seven games remaining, but the numbers increasingly point toward an offseason of difficult decisions rather than a postseason run.

Following Monday night's 23-20 loss to the previously winless Los Angeles Chargers, Baltimore has an 8.6% chance of landing a top-five pick in the 2027 NFL draft, compared with a 4.1% chance of reaching the playoffs, according to ESPN Analytics figures reported by ESPN's Jamison Hensley. Those are separate projections, not complementary outcomes, but the comparison captures how sharply Baltimore's season has turned.

The Ravens dropped to 4-6 with their fifth consecutive defeat after starting 4-1. Their first five-game losing streak since 2021 has left little room for another missed opportunity.

Baltimore sits 14th in the current draft order, according to the Tankathon standings supplied Tuesday. That is considerably different from being projected to select in the top five: ESPN's 8.6% figure measures the possibility of Baltimore finishing in that range, not its current position. Tennessee, the Chargers and Miami occupy the first three spots at 1-8, followed by the New York Jets at 2-8 and Philadelphia at 2-7. Baltimore is grouped with Dallas and Las Vegas at 4-6, with strength of schedule separating their positions.

The Ravens are not positioned for a premium selection yet. However, five consecutive losses have made draft position a relevant part of the discussion with nearly half their schedule remaining.

Jacoby Brissett provided a lift in his Ravens debut, completing 33 of 49 passes for 340 yards with an interception. It was not enough to overcome a running game that produced 16 yards on 15 attempts and a defense that surrendered 160 rushing yards.

Derrick Henry scored twice but finished with 10 yards on 10 carries. Baltimore's final opportunity ended when Chargers defensive back Tarheeb Still stripped Zay Flowers after a reception, and Elijah Molden recovered. The performance underscored why a quarterback change alone cannot rescue the season. Baltimore must generate movement up front, defend the run and protect possessions to turn improved passing production into victories.

Baltimore remains mathematically alive and can finish 11-6 by winning out. Getting there would require an immediate reversal from a team that is 0-2 against AFC North opponents and 2-5 within the conference. The next opportunity comes Sunday at Carolina, followed by a trip to Houston before the Ravens' bye.

For now, the distinction is straightforward: Baltimore still has a path to the playoffs, but ESPN's projections give the Ravens a better chance of selecting among the first five teams in April than playing postseason football in January.

Herbert, who injured his left hand in last week’s loss to the Texans but wasn’t on the injury report this week, went 6-for-9 for 91 yards in the first half. He also ran for 45 yards on six carries.

Hampton has 36 yards on 10 carries, and Tre’ Harris has five catches for 86 yards.

Jacoby Brissett, making his first start for the Ravens after the trade from Arizona, has completed 14 of 23 passes for 163 yards and an interception. Del’Shawn Phillips had the pick on a pass that was behind Ja’Kobi Lane and deflected to the Chargers linebacker.

Derrick Henry has a 2-yard touchdown run, but he has minus-1 yard on five carries. Mark Andrews has four catches for 41 yards.

#2781. Four things to watch as Sixers look to respond against Raptors

Four things to watch as Sixers look to respond against Raptors originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers will not need to wait long for an opportunity to move past their most lopsided loss of the season.

Less than 48 hours after Boston's 123-101 win exposed several of Philadelphia's early weaknesses, the Sixers will host the Toronto Raptors on Thursday night at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

The Raptors are coming off a difficult meeting with the Celtics, too. Toronto led Boston by 22 points Wednesday before being outscored 61-34 over the final 29:24 of a 98-93 loss.

Philadelphia enters at 9-3 following Tuesday's defeat. Toronto is 5-6 and will complete a back-to-back and its third game in four nights.

LeBron James has been ruled out because of low-back and sciatica management. Joel Embiid is questionable with right plantar fascia injury management. Toronto will be without Kawhi Leonard, Scottie Barnes and Nate Bittle.

Here are four areas to watch as the Sixers try to respond.

Few teams have forced opponents into mistakes as frequently as Toronto.

The Raptors have generated 18.9 turnovers per game, the highest mark in the NBA. They are also averaging 10.9 steals and nearly 15 offensive rebounds.

The Sixers have countered that type of pressure well. Their 13.0 turnovers per game are tied for one of the lowest marks in the league.

Toronto's length remains disruptive even without Leonard and Barnes. The Raptors regularly crowd ball handlers, reach into passing lanes and send multiple players toward the offensive glass. Jakob Poeltl supplies size inside while RJ Barrett, Ja'Kobe Walter and Collin Murray-Boyles can create problems away from the ball.

The Raptors' pressure can punish a careless pass twice. It takes away a Sixers possession and gives Toronto an opportunity to score before the defense gets organized.

Philadelphia has largely avoided those mistakes while adjusting to a new lineup and playing at a quicker pace. Maintaining that control without James, who has handled a significant share of the playmaking, will be one of Thursday's clearest challenges.

Toronto has not built its offense around half-court shooting.

The Raptors are averaging 49.6 paint points and 16.4 fast-break points. Even while shorthanded Wednesday, they scored 18 points in transition against Boston.

That presents a direct test for a Sixers defense allowing 16.5 fast-break points per game, the fourth-highest figure in the NBA. Philadelphia has also surrendered 49.5 paint points per contest.

The first concern will be slowing the ball.

Immanuel Quickley returned Wednesday after missing two games with right hamstring soreness. He gives Toronto another guard capable of attacking early in the shot clock, while Barrett has consistently looked to get downhill with Leonard and Barnes unavailable.

Poeltl can create scoring chances without having a play called for him. He runs the floor, screens for Toronto's guards, rolls toward the rim and cleans up misses.

The Sixers have been effective in transition themselves, averaging 17.1 fast-break points. Maxey and VJ Edgecombe can change ends quickly after a stop, but Philadelphia must secure the ball first.

Thursday's game could swing on which team gets to run and which one is forced to create against a set defense.

Toronto provided an extreme example Wednesday of how quickly the three-point line can alter a game.

The Raptors entered their matchup against Boston shooting 31.8 percent from beyond the arc. They proceeded to hit 10 of their 18 attempts in the first quarter, tying a franchise record for threes in a period.

They went 6 for 25 over the final three quarters.

After Wednesday, Toronto is shooting 32.4 percent from three this season. The Sixers should not treat the Raptors like an elite perimeter team. They also cannot allow them to find early confidence through uncovered catch-and-shoot attempts.

Walter made five threes Wednesday and scored a team-high 17 points. Rookie Allen Graves added 13 points, while Quickley, Barrett and Jamison Battle are all willing shooters when the ball reaches them in rhythm.

Philadelphia's problems against Boston extended beyond the Celtics making difficult shots. The Sixers were late on several closeouts and occasionally allowed too much separation while navigating screens.

Toronto does not rely on the three as heavily as Boston. Wednesday showed it can still build a lead quickly when those shots start falling.

The Raptors expended significant energy Wednesday before traveling to Philadelphia.

They scored 38 first-quarter points, built a 22-point lead and then watched Boston erase the entire margin before the end of the third quarter. Quickley and Barrett each played more than 31 minutes. Poeltl and Walter both played at least 29.

The Sixers should have the fresher legs. Whether they take advantage could become apparent early.

Philadelphia has averaged 26.3 first-quarter points with a minus-2.2 scoring margin in opening periods. Slow starts have forced the Sixers to play from behind several times, including in their 29-point comeback against Cleveland.

An aggressive start would make Toronto respond quickly after a draining loss. Maxey and Edgecombe can apply pressure by pushing the ball after defensive rebounds instead of allowing the Raptors to set their pressure defense.

James' absence will require an adjustment. Maxey, Brown and Anfernee Simons should handle more of the ball and more of the decisions that come with initiating the offense.

If Embiid plays, the Sixers can make Toronto work through Poeltl by establishing him inside. If Embiid sits, Philadelphia's size concerns will become more pronounced with Adem Bona, Dominick Barlow and Ariel Hukporti also unavailable.

The Sixers have a rest advantage against a depleted opponent coming off an emotional loss. Their response should begin well before the fourth quarter.

The gap between first and second place is widening, and Week 10 featured one of the biggest NFC South matchups of the year.

The Bucs were on their bye week, and after not playing, they are in second place in the division. Next week, the Bucs will be facing the Lions, but QB Baker Mayfield's availability is up in the air.

To our only winner of the week, the Panthers are now the only team with four wins in the division and sit atop the NFC South. They were forced to go away from Bijan Robinson in this matchup against the Kansas City Chiefs, and eventhough Michael Penix finished with 286 passing yards, the passing game couldn't score a touchdown. Well, both are true right now. Kellen Moore, even after firing T.J. Paganetti, still hasn't figured out the run game, even against the Panthers' run defense. As for Shough, he is playing with a broken hand and is probably playing as bad as we have seen from him. He is missing wide-open throws, not seeing wide-open receivers, and is playing scared in the pocket. This is why it was so important for Moore to figure out the run game and offensive line this past offseason. Keeping Shough a bit more comfortable in the pocket while also freeing up the passing game would cause Shough to improve. We have seen what Shough can do, and that's good enough for him to be a starter, but he needs a new head coach.
