#2739. Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid

Observations after Brown-Maxey duo scores 58 points in Sixers' win without Embiid originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Jaylen Brown-Tyrese Maxey duo starred, LeBron James reached another milestone, and the Sixers earned a win without Joel Embiid on Sunday afternoon.

They improved to 11-3 on the season with a 131-124 victory over the Pacers at Xfinity Mobile Arena.

Brown scored 31 points and Maxey posted 27.

LeBron James had 15 points and 11 assists. He passed Jason Kidd on Sunday for third in NBA history with 12,092 assists.

Pascal Siakam scored 31 points for the 5-9 Pacers.

On the first day of a back-to-back, the Sixers were down Embiid (right foot plantar fascia injury management), Dominick Barlow (lower back stress reaction), Adem Bona (cervical spine sprain) and Ariel Hukporti (right Achilles tendon tear). The Pacers' injured players included Tyrese Haliburton and Andrew Nembhard.

The Sixers will face the Nets on Monday night in Brooklyn. Here are observations on their win over the Pacers:

Jabari Walker started for the first time this season, opening the game at center. He's been in the Sixers' rotation the past five games since Bona's injury.

Walker set up the Sixers' first basket, swiping a steal and then tossing the ball ahead to Maxey for a fast-break dunk. Maxey and Brown both began well and combined to score all of the Sixers' first 15 points.

Though he conceded five inches to 7-foot Pacers center Ivica Zubac, Walker was solid in his first stint. He got an early post stop on Zubac and then snatched a strong defensive rebound.

Dean Wade was next up for the Sixers at the five. He sunk a three-pointer on his first shot to give the team a 20-16 lead.

Walker ended up playing 25 minutes and posting 11 points and seven rebounds. Going back to the preseason, his stock has steadily risen.

"Here we are again after another game saying Jabari played very well," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "He plays hard, he rebounds, he's got another level of confidence with his shooting this year.

"It's kind of wash, rinse and repeat again with him (today). He was great. … Jabari went in there and battled (Zubac). I thought he certainly matched him, if not outplayed him, which is a heck of a job."

Wade had 12 points and seven boards in 35 minutes. Overall, the Sixers' lack of healthy conventional centers is still a clear deficiency. Of course, everything should be simpler once Embiid is back in action.

The Sixers' defense did not have a sparkling start. The Pacers tallied 35 points in the first quarter and shot 62.5 percent from the field.

Brown helped the Sixers avoid a substantial deficit by draining all sorts of jumpers. He rarely appears bothered by close contests. Brown moved to 7 for 9 from the floor when he knocked down a three early in the second quarter. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope's triple knotted the contest up at 37-all.

Nurse needed to stray from his Plan A rotation when VJ Edgecombe picked up his third foul with 9:56 left in the second quarter. Labaron Philon Jr., who got a game with the G League's Delaware Blue Coats on Friday night, subbed in.

For a rookie guard, Philon has looked comfortable and confident on offense in most of his NBA appearances. He swished a long-range jumper in the second quarter and saw a floater rim out.

Brown reached the 20-point mark and put the Sixers up one with a slam assisted by James late in the second quarter.

He ran into serious foul trouble in the third quarter. Brown was whistled for his fourth foul with 9:53 left in the period for an off-arm shove on Aaron Nesmith. Wade entered and Brown exited.

The Sixers' slim lead didn't slip away. Walker nailed threes on consecutive possessions and Maxey jammed in a dunk.

Soon after, Tacko Fall came in for his first meaningful minutes as a Sixer. The 7-foot-6 two-way contract player grabbed an offensive board off a missed Edgecombe free throw, which drew one of the day's loudest cheers. With Fall on the floor, Maxey and James connected on an alley-oop that extended the Sixers' lead to 82-73.

When the Sixers tried to sit in a zone, Fall was called for a defensive three-second violation. Whatever the scheme, most of Fall's defensive work will come around the paint. He certainly has exceptional physical traits as a rim protector and is not nimble on the perimeter.

Fall logged five scoreless third-quarter minutes and recorded three rebounds. The Sixers outscored Indiana by a point with Fall on the court. Given Fall is occupying a (two-way) roster slot and the Sixers are so depleted at center, it made sense to see a bit of what he can do outside of garbage time. While five minutes isn't enough for any grand conclusions, it's very safe to say Fall is a unique player.

"I'm 7-6, so I can change the game in so many ways," Fall said. "Offensively, setting screens, making sure guys get open, being an easy target for lobs. Defensively, I feel like I've always been an elite rim protector and I've worked on my body a lot to be able to move a lot better."

Nurse praised Fall's outing, saying he "gave us some great minutes and a nice lift."

Fall is appreciative of his first NBA opportunity since the 2021-22 season.

"I've worked very hard," he said. "God has brought me back here and I'm thankful. And I'm ready. Whenever my name is called, I'm going to go out there and do my job."

The Sixers built their advantage to 102-87 with a Brown three early in the fourth quarter. They couldn't cruise to a blowout, though.

Siakam kept hurting the Sixers as a driver and had a highly efficient fourth quarter, scoring 19 points on 7-for-9 shooting. Quenton Jackson's layup trimmed Indiana's deficit to 119-115.

The Sixers ultimately held off the Pacers' push.

Caldwell-Pope canned a three, Wade threw down an and-one dunk, and James checked off his milestone assist with a late dish to Edgecombe for a layup.

Cousins had been playing at a high level for the Raiders before being shipped back to Minnesota, and will look to elevate this offense upon his homecoming. He will get his next chance to do so on Sunday Night Football from Mexico, as he akes on the San Francisco 49ers.

#2737. Derrick Henry on 10 carries for 10 yards: I don't have an answer, just not good enough

With the Ravens starting a quarterback, Jacoby Brissett, who had been on the team for less than two weeks, they hoped to get good production from running back Derrick Henry on Monday night. That didn’t happen.

Henry gained just 10 yards on 10 carries, and another 10 yards on five catches. Afterward he said he couldn’t explain what went wrong in a 23-20 loss to the Chargers.

“I don’t know. I don’t have no answer. Guys who are out there are healthy, trying to do their jobs, we just ain’t good enough,” Henry said.

Overall, the Ravens totaled 15 carries for 16 yards, their lowest rushing yardage output in more than 10 years. Henry did run for two touchdowns, but he also had five plays on which he got no gain or negative yardage. Asked if the absence of quarterback Lamar Jackson is the problem, Henry said the players who are there have to take responsibility.

“We just haven’t been executing. Of course Lamar is a big part of what we do, but it’s a grown-man league,” Henry said. “We just haven’t been doing our jobs good enough, it hasn’t been good enough, and I think losing five in a row shows that. Of course having 8 would help, but at the end of the day, we’ve got to go out there and do our jobs.”

The Ravens are 4-6 and the season appears to be slipping away, but Henry said he’ll always believe they can turn the season around.

“I’m never not gonna believe that we can turn it around and play the way we want to play,” Henry said. “You’ve still got to go to work. The season is still going to be here, you’ve still got to play. None of us are going to be like, ‘Let’s lose the rest of them.’ You’ve still got to do your best.”

#2736. Fantasy Football 2026: Waiver wire, Week 11 edition

Welcome to the Fantasy Football Waiver Wire for Week 11.

The waiver wire was a feeding frenzy last week. A fresh round of injuries, a few NFL trades that impacted rosters, four teams on bye, and a lot of attractive free agents on offer were chum in the water, and managers bit.

Well, buckle up, fantasy players, because Week 11 is the worst bye week of the season. It's the only one with six teams on bye, and you're going to hear terms like "byepocalypse". On top of that, there's less to choose from this week, in part because so many players got scooped up last week (examples: Emmett Johnson, Kaleb Johnson, and Tank Dell). But that doesn't mean there aren't players to add who can help you. I'll do my best to identify them here.

The Process: Each week, I try to identify the top Waiver Wire targets and evaluate the short- and long-term prospects of those players. I also do my best to reveal which players who are coming off a big week are fool's gold. Finally, I look at streamers who might help you in a pinch (this is usually more important during bye weeks).

Some players discussed won't be available in your league. I try to list players who are available in more than 50% of leagues. But what does that even mean? A 10-team league with six bench spots will have a much larger Free Agent pool than a 14-team league with eight. Most leagues fall somewhere in between. This isn't "one size fits all." By way of example, I won't list Kirk Cousins, Emmett Johnson, Kaleb Johnson, Antonio Williams, Jordyn Tyson, Tank Dell, or Ryan Flournoy, among others who probably aren't available in any of your leagues.

A slight change this week: this column is dropping AFTER the Week 11 Monday night game (LAC@BAL).

WEEK 11 BYES: Falcons, Browns, Packers, Rams, Patriots, Seahawks

Don't look now, but Cam Ward is starting to look more like a No. 1 draft pick and less like a lemon. He's been putting up fantasy points (46 of them over his last two games), and doing it without his No. 1 receiver, rookie Carnell Tate. Tennessee has scored at least 23 points in four straight games. We saw flashes last season, but Ward hasn't shown that he can be a consistent producer. His upcoming schedule is exceptional, with games against Dallas, Washington, Las Vegas, Indianapolis, and Detroit in the next month and a half. He's a nice add in 2-QB leagues if he's available, or if you just lost Drake Maye and need emergency help.

Fernando Mendoza (who I recommended last week) had his moments in his debut. Let's give him a break, since it was against Seattle, and next week he gets the equally tough Broncos (and he might be without Brock Bowers), rested off a bye. Not an easy way to start a career. But I think he can help you out if you need a second quarterback in a Superflex down the stretch. He's not afraid to run, which is a plus.

Need a Week 11 streamer? A lot of people do with the big bye week. It's not a great week for that. Ward is your best bet, against that atrocious Dallas defense that gave up 46 more points in Week 10. Jacoby Brissett at the Panthers is in play as well. He attempted 49 passes on Monday night while throwing for 340 yards. And I'll actually mention Justin Herbert, who gets the Jets at home.

Beyond those let me show you our bargain basement. Geno Smith (assuming he's able to play this week) is at the Chargers. J.J. McCarthy has a tough home matchup against the Jags. Baker Mayfield or Jalon Daniels at the Lions isn't terrible. Kyle McCord could have the gift of extended garbage time against backups at Buffalo, and especially with the Bills set to play the Chiefs on Thanksgiving night. That's a lot to talk yourself into. Speaking of which, Joe Milton wasn't good in his first start against the Giants, but he did run for 64 yards on nine carries and the Eagles could finally get some weapons back coming out of their bye. They host the Steelers, whose defense never got off the plane in Cincinnati on Sunday night. Hey, it's a brutal week and desperate times and all that…

Emari Demercado will be a hot add this week, as he has a good shot to be the lead back for the Texans on Thursday night in a plus-matchup against the Colts. Houston hasn't run the ball effectively all season, but with Woody Marks unlikely to get out of concussion protocol on a short week and David Montgomery iffy to return to action, this is a no-brainer, even if it's a short-term pickup. But don't go crazy with a FAAB bid.

Seth McGowan may have been dropped by many speculators after Jonathan Taylor didn't get traded. McGowan should be rostered. He saw a season-high 12 carries in Week 10 and scored a TD for the second straight week. He's part of the offense.

I can't imagine many people dropped Kyle Monangai, but since he never went on IR it's possible that some managers needed the roster space. He's a priority add if available, and a great streaming option this week against the Saints, who allow the most fantasy points to running backs. David Montgomery is probably still rostered in most places (people are stubborn with their fourth- or fifth-round picks, and he did score thrice in Week 1), but I'd look to add him if he isn't.

Backs who should be rostered: Kaelon Black, Kenneth Gainwell, Justice Hill, Samaje Perine, Dylan Sampson,and Israel Ibanikanda (only because the Cowboys have no depth at the position).

The Breakfast Club, a/k/a/ the "don't you forget about me" list: Jonathon Brooks, Jacory Croskey-Merritt, R.J. Harvey, and Tank Bigsby. Most of these are still eligible for an IR spot.

Xavier Hutchinson tops the list this week. The Texans have a lot of bodies at the position (and especially once Marvin Harrison, Jr. returns), but for now Hutchinson is operating as the No. 2 receiver and he has scored in three straight games. The Texans are airing it out (because, again, they can't run the ball and their vaunted defense is struggling), making Hutchinson an attractive option.

Pat Bryant may have made it through last week's waivers because Denver had a bye in Week 10. Bo Nix favors him, and it's unclear if Jaylen Waddle will be back in Week 11. Even with a healthy Waddle, Bryant has value. He's right there with Hutchinson for me.

Caleb Douglas made the list last week, and I'll add feature him again, even though Kyle McCord doesn't inspire a ton of confidence. Douglas is the receiver to roster from Miami. He's getting good target volume and topped 70 yards in each of the last two games. This week's game at Buffalo should have extended garbage time, as discussed above.

Need depth? I'll repeat a bunch of names I've listed in recent weeks, together with some new ones: Keenan Allen, Konata Mumpfield, Malachi Fields, Tre' Harris, Laquon Treadwell, Jordan Watkins, and Jakobi Meyers.

Is Brissett going to turn Mark Andrews into Trey McBride? No, of course not. But Andrews got onto the fantasy radar on Monday night with significant volume and production (8-82-0 and he caught all of his targets). Given the lack of quality at the position, he's worth a pickup as the Ravens don't have any reliable receivers after Zay Flowers, and you know Brissett likes to get ball to his tight end.

I'll just keep running back the same names. Mike Gesicki, Darren Waller, Tyler Higbee, and Pat Freiermuth are all boom-bust options who can get you a TD (and maybe a little more) if you hit it right. Chig Okonkwo offers more consistency. Brevyn Spann-Ford is interesting, given how much Dallas throws. Terrance Ferguson is a stash if you've got the space. Michael Mayer should see a bump in volume if Bowers misses time.

#2735. Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn

Inside Sixers-Nets: How Philly can finish a back-to-back sweep in Brooklyn originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

The Sixers will arrive in Brooklyn with one victory already secured on their back-to-back and a different combination of stars likely available for the second.

Philadelphia improved to 11-3 with a 131-124 win over the Pacers on Sunday. Jaylen Brown scored a season-high 31 points, Tyrese Maxey added 27 and LeBron James recorded 11 assists to move past Jason Kidd for third in NBA history.

James has not played both ends of a back-to-back this season, making it reasonable to expect him to sit Monday night. Joel Embiid, meanwhile, rested Sunday and is expected to return against Brooklyn. The Sixers had not submitted their official injury report as of Monday morning.

The Nets enter at 3-9, but this is not the same roster that finished 20-62 last season. Before the Sixers attempt to win twice in two nights, here is a closer look at what has changed in Brooklyn and where Monday's matchup could turn.

Nic Claxton is now in Chicago. Julius Randle arrived from Minnesota as the central piece of a four-team trade. Keon Ellis and Moe Wagner joined in free agency, while Michael Porter Jr. remains one of Brooklyn's primary scoring options.

Porter is available after missing Saturday's game for personal reasons. He will give the Nets another shooter after Brooklyn struggled badly without him in a 129-110 loss to Memphis.

The Nets' rotation also includes a large group of young players. Egor Dëmin, Nolan Traore and Mikel Brown Jr. have all received minutes in the backcourt, while Noah Clowney, Danny Wolf and Day'Ron Sharpe have played in the frontcourt.

The combination has produced uneven results. Brooklyn averages 110.2 points and allows 116.4 per game. Its minus-6.3 net rating ranks 23rd in the league.

Brooklyn has shown its offensive ceiling. The Nets scored 145 points in a 34-point win over New Orleans last week. Three nights later, they fell behind Memphis by 26 and lost by 19.

Jordi Fernandez has been direct about the difference between the team he sees in practice and the one that has appeared in games.

"We practice better than we play," the Nets head coach said after Saturday's loss. "It's a frustrating sign because you believe you're better."

The Nets did not merely miss shots against Memphis. Their problems began with their approach.

"We didn't punch first," Randle said. "Just organization. I think maybe just trying too hard, playing a little unorganized, stuff like that. And then just attention to detail. I think we've got a lapse of focus sometimes."

Fernandez was even more direct when discussing his young guards.

"They've got to grow fast," he said. "Being 20 is not an excuse."

That inexperience has appeared in Brooklyn's ball control. The Nets' offensive turnover rate ranks 28th in the league.

They have been much more disruptive on the other end. Brooklyn has forced turnovers on 13.9 percent of its opponents' possessions, tied for seventh.

The Sixers forced 10 Indiana turnovers and converted them into 19 points Sunday. Philadelphia committed 13 total turnovers while playing at the quick tempo it has emphasized since training camp.

Brooklyn has enough length and athleticism to disrupt the Sixers. It has not consistently maintained its own organization after creating that pressure.

Brooklyn has allowed 14.2 three-pointers per game, the eighth-most in the NBA. Opponents have posted a 55.2 effective field-goal percentage against the Nets.

That presents another opportunity for a Sixers team receiving strong outside shooting from several role players.

Anfernee Simons ranks third in the league with 33 three-pointers off the bench and is shooting 41.8 percent from deep. Kentavious Caldwell-Pope has made 46.5 percent of his threes, while Dean Wade is at 41.4 percent.

Maxey made five threes against Indiana and Brown added three. Jabari Walker also continued his strong start by making three of his six attempts from deep.

Those contributions matter on the second night of a back-to-back, especially if James sits. Philadelphia should not need Maxey and Brown to create every possession from a standstill if its shooters continue punishing open space.

Embiid's expected return would change the shape of Philadelphia's offense after James served as its primary organizer Sunday.

Brooklyn no longer has Claxton to use against Embiid. Sharpe has moved into a larger role and is coming off 16 points and 11 rebounds in Memphis. Wagner, Clowney and Randle give Fernandez several other bodies to try, but none presents the same defensive profile as Claxton.

Sharpe's work on the glass deserves attention. The Sixers have survived significant rebounding deficits this season, but allowing Brooklyn extra possessions would give Porter and Randle more chances to settle into the game.

On the other end, Embiid should have opportunities to force Brooklyn's frontcourt into difficult decisions. Sending help would create openings for Philadelphia's shooters. Defending him individually would leave Sharpe, Wagner and Clowney with a demanding assignment.

The Sixers have already won three games without James. Monday could require them to do it again, this time with Embiid returning as the center of the offense.

Brooklyn's record suggests a favorable matchup. Its shooting and young talent make it less comfortable than the standings indicate.

The Sixers handled the first half of their back-to-back by producing a season-high 131 points. Finishing the sweep will require them to adjust to a different available roster while preventing an inconsistent Nets team from finding one of its explosive nights.

#2734. Von Miller blames himself, not the play call, for Christian McCaffrey's late TD

The Cowboys pulled within 38-32 of the 49ers with 3:42 left in the fourth quarter on Sunday. Only 19 seconds later, Christian McCaffrey was celebrating in the end zone.

Cowboys defensive coordinator Christian Parker is under fire for calling cover zero on second-and-10. He sent a six-man blitz, leaving man-to-man coverage and Von Miller matched up with McCaffrey.

McCaffrey called it “a perfect look for that play.”

The running back ran a seam route, quickly got a step (or two or three) on the 37-year-old edge rusher, caught the ball at the 40 and was off to the races. Miller reached 18.5 mph while chasing McCaffrey, but it wasn’t fast enough.

Miller, though, defended Parker and the play call on Monday.

“As a defensive coordinator in that situation, you want to be aggressive,” Miller said, via Nick Harris of the Fort Worth Star-Telegram. “We were trying to win the game. We were trying to create momentum. We were down by six points. If we could have just got off the field. . . . As a defensive coordinator, you want to be aggressive and want to win the game. That’s the type of mindset you want in these games.”

Parker is three years younger than Miller, but has Miller’s full support despite the struggles of the Cowboys defense. Dallas allowed 584 yards to the 49ers.

“Nah, that’s crazy,” Miller said of the calls for Parker’s job. “Christian is an incredible leader of men. All of these offensive and defensive coordinators are geniuses. It’s really getting your players to buy in. What happened with me yesterday is a microcosm of what has happened the whole season. Instead of me looking for a simpler way to do that job, I have to find a better way to do it. That had nothing to do with Christian Parker. I had been coached on how to play that route. I’ve had examples of that route, went over that play in practice this week. I just didn’t make that play. That had nothing to do with Christian Parker. I just didn’t execute.”

#2733. Lions' coach defends player celebrations after Aidan Hutchinson ACL tear

The Detroit Lions will be without their biggest star on defense for the rest of the year.

Lions head coach Dan Campbell confirmed that edge rusher Aidan Hutchinson's knee injury, which he sustained celebrating a sack on Sunday, Nov. 15, was a torn ACL. Hutchinson will miss the remainder of the season as he recovers.

"Man, what a gut punch," Campbell said on Detroit radio station 97.1 The Ticket on Tuesday, Nov. 17. "He's such a phenomenal player. This guy's an impact player, but more importantly he's an unbelievable teammate. And he's one of our captains. It just stinks, man."

The coach also said that Hutchinson's injury will not create any discussions with the Lions' players when it comes to celebrating. Campbell said he doesn't want the team to stop playing with emotion just because Hutchinson's injury came while celebrating.

"I think what happened speaks for itself," Campbell said. "We're not talking to 8-year-olds here."

He explained that he once felt as though he tore a bicep while pumping his arm in celebration, but it hasn't stopped him from showing emotion.

"This game is built off of emotion," Campbell said. "And if you have to contain yourself after a play is made – I'm not talking about in the moment, in the game, because you gotta keep your head – then you're really becoming something you're not."

"I'm not going to say, 'Nobody celebrates anymore.'"

More: Aidan Hutchinson's injury was a freak accident – and totally avoidable

Campbell confirmed that Hutchinson tore his ACL while celebrating his sack of Drake Maye. The Nov. 15 injury will knock the edge rusher out for the remainder of the season.

"I hate it, I know he hates it," Campbell said. "But I mean, the number of moves that I've seen Hutch make and the number of plyometrics, explosive movements, the spins, the one leg lateral movements, lateral balances, I'm just like, it was one of those things, man," Campbell said.

Hutchinson sustained the injury when he landed after a celebratory leap. The edge rusher's right knee buckled when his legs hit the ground again, and he fell over and grabbed at his knee.

#2731. Three referees will be working in other roles on Sunday, due to the ground stop

The full scope of the adjustments to the usual officiating crews isn’t officially known. It’s clear, however, that Friday’s nationwide ground stop will result in significant changes to the usual groupings of officiating crews.

On Saturday morning, the NFL announced that three non-referees will serve as referees for four Week 10 games, with one of them — Don Willard — working as the referee for the 1:00 p.m. ET game between the Dolphins and Colts before traveling to Cincinnati for Sunday night’s Steelers-Bengals game.

Via Ben Austro of FootballZebras.com, three referees will be working in other roles on Sunday.

Referee Carl Cheffers and referee Alex Kemp will work as umpires. Referee Shawn Hochuli (pictured) will work as a back judge.

The reconfiguration resulted from the NFL’s decision to assign officials to games within driving distance of their homes.

Austro also notes that the replay officials for all Sunday games will work out of NFL headquarters. They were in Detroit on Friday for an all-day, in-person training session. Instead of fanning out to the various game locations, they all went to New York.

It sets the stage for a potentially strange day, with any mistakes inevitably being blamed on the inability of the NFL to get the officials where they needed to be. If it works, however, it could prompt the NFL to reconsider the overall staffing plan, if there’s a way to do it that reduces expenses.

I think that’s a tongue-in-cheek remark. I hope it’s a tongue-in-cheek remark.

Either way, Sunday could get interesting. The players and coaches have gotten to where they’re supposed to be. The officials haven’t, and the deck has been dramatically reshuffled in order to ensure that each game will be properly staffed.

#2730. Sixers, Brown have a ‘humbling' night in loss to Celtics

Sixers, Brown have a 'humbling' night in loss to Celtics originally appeared on NBC Sports Philadelphia

Whatever Jaylen Brown and the Sixers dreamed up for their first matchup against the Celtics soon flew out the window on Tuesday night.

"I think that we've got some guys that are obviously shooting much lower percentages than their last season or their career average — almost a handful of guys," Sixers head coach Nick Nurse said. "And I don't think it's because they're shooting fallaway, totally contested ones. I think they're shots we want them to take. So I think they'll continue to take 'em and I'm pretty sure those will even out into their career averages."

Joel Embiid is at 37 percent from the floor and 4 for 33 from three-point range (12.1 percent).

The Sixers' seven-time All-Star center got through an injury scare when he rolled his ankle late in the second quarter and hit a three early in the third. It was his first long-range make since the Sixers' home opener back on Oct. 22.

"It's a work in progress," Embiid said. "I think the biggest thing is just not thinking. I'm probably overthinking it. The rhythm of the game is different. I'm so used to having to create for myself, so it's a little bit different.

"But I think I'm slowly getting better. You could see as soon as I made a three, the next shot in the short corner … I didn't even think about it. I just shot it and let it fly, and I made it. … I think it's just about taking what's in front of you. Make or miss, keep working on your game. It's going to get there at some point."

Even with the Sixers second in the Eastern Conference standings at 9-3, Embiid believes the team's defense has "slacked off a little bit" in recent games.

"I don't really look at the record," he said. "I just look at the process of, 'Are we getting better every day?' We can always do a better job at all phases of the game."

"They're one of the top teams in the league and they've been together for a while," Brown said. "We've got a lot to work on over here, so we've got to get to work."

The Sixers will face the Raptors on Thursday night in Philadelphia.

They'll have to wait until Jan. 21 in Boston to see the Celtics again.

"I was looking forward to this," Brown said. "I was looking forward to playing well, but s— happens. Just get ready for the next one."

#2728. Through 10 weeks, the AFC playoff field is basically set

The 2021 expansion of the regular season to 17 games has given the bad teams more time to separate from the good teams. Which makes it easier for the eventual playoff teams to declare themselves.

This year, the seven AFC playoff teams have essentially declared themselves after only 10 weeks of games.

The most important developments in that regard were these: (1) the Browns beating the Texans; (2) the Ravens losing to the Chargers; and (3) the Patriots losing badly to the Lions — and losing quarterback Drake Maye, possibly for the rest of the season.

Already, we know that the Bills, Jaguars, Bengals, Broncos, and Chiefs are essentially locks to win tickets to the postseason party. The Steelers, despite being outclassed by the Bengals, have enough winnable games remaining to land a wild-card berth.

The only spot up for grabs is the seven seed. And the Browns are now in the driver’s seat.

Already, four AFC teams are dead in the water: the 2-8 Jets, the bill1-8 Dolphins, the 1-8 Titans, and the 1-8 Chargers. The 3-7 Colts barely have a pulse, and it won’t last long. That’s five of 16 teams, with a fork already stuck in a spot where forks aren’t supposed to go. And the 3-6 Texans aren’t far behind.

The remaining contenders for the last spot in the playoff tree are the 6-4 Browns, the 5-5 Patriots, the 4-6 Raiders, and the 4-6 Ravens. New England could be in the process of imploding. The Raiders are 1-6 over their last seven games, and they’re now starting a rookie quarterback. The Ravens have lost five in a row.

As a result, there’s currently not much drama as to who the seven AFC playoff teams will be.

The good news is that the top of the seven-team hierarchy remains undecided — and it could stay that way for a while. The Bills, Jaguars, and Bengals stand apart from the rest. The Broncos and Chiefs remain in the mix. (The Steelers seem to be destined for another one-and-done postseason appearance, with a road game looming to Buffalo, Jacksonville, Cincinnati, or Denver/Kansas City.)

The bad news, and the inescapable reality, is that the have-nots have become crystal clear. With eight weeks of the season left.

For a league that loves parity, there isn’t much of it when it comes to the line separating the playoff teams from the wait-til-next-year franchises.

#2727. North Carolina guard Neo Avdalas to miss about 2 months after suffering a broken wrist

CHAPEL HILL, N.C. (AP) — North Carolina guard Neo Avdalas is expected to miss about two months after needing surgery for a broken left wrist.

The team announced Avdalas' injury Tuesday. Losing the versatile Avdalas is a blow for first-year coach Michael Malone's team, with the 6-foot-9 sophomore transfer from Virginia Tech averaging 9.0 points while also serving as a facilitator by averaging a team-high 3.8 assists.

Avdalas was hurt during Friday's home loss to Georgia after falling to the floor on a second-half drive. He was seen holding his left hand after getting back on defense.

The Tar Heels (3-1) host Marshall on Friday.

#2726. NFL Panic Meter: Should Steelers be worried after embarrassing blowout loss?

One bad game shouldn't eliminate eight games of solid football.

But that was a seriously bad game for the Pittsburgh Steelers.

Get out-gained 337-40 in a half and there will be some reasons for concern. The Steelers were absolutely blown out by the Cincinnati Bengals, and it didn't help that it happened on a big Sunday night stage. The Steelers were 6-2 going into the game and while there was skepticism over their staying power, nobody expected them to get destroyed like that.

The Steelers don't seem too worried about it ruining their season. Aaron Rodgers handled a question about whether the loss would change the trajectory of the season with, "That's very dramatic," and head coach Mike McCarthy took a similar tack.

"It's one loss and it will be dealt with as such," McCarthy said, via the team's site. "I'm more focused on the details of the fundamentals and the things we needed to do. We're not going to drive down the drama road. It's a waste of time. It's a waste of energy. We're going to pour our energy into ourselves, look in the mirror and stay focused on the things that we can control."

Cincinnati Bengals inside linebacker Oren Burks brings down Pittsburgh Steelers running back Jaylen Warren.

That's a fine approach, because there's nothing else McCarthy can say. But the Steelers have some real concerns. They came into the season tied for the oldest roster in the NFL, with many older veterans starting with 42-year-old quarterback Aaron Rodgers. It's a long season and the Steelers have to worry about injury and fatigue setting in. The Steelers have gotten very little out of their receivers this season, which limits the explosiveness of the offense, and while the defense has been good it was picked apart by Joe Burrow.

But it's not time to get too worried. The Steelers had shown significant improvement before having a horrendous night in Cincinnati. That isn't all erased, nor are six wins in what is becoming a very soft AFC wild-card race. Rodgers was good enough with the limitations at receiver through eight games and while Sunday night started with him throwing incomplete on his first seven passes for the first time in his career, there haven't been signs of a steep decline before Sunday night. It could happen in the second half of the season but there's no clear and imminent danger of it.

Good teams can have bad games. The Bengals are hot. It happens. As McCarthy said, there's no reason to go down a drama road. The Steelers should be fine. But that's a little less certain after a truly miserable night in Cincinnati.

Panic meter: Let's not give up on the Steelers yet, but remember Sunday night as a turning point if things do go south.

When Drake Maye was sacked by Aidan Hutchinson, Patriots and Lions fans had no idea both teams' seasons were changing on that play.

The Patriots are concerned that Maye fractured his ankle, and we'll get to Hutchinson in a bit. It appears Maye is either going to miss significant time or the rest of the season. That's terrible news for the 5-5 Patriots less than a week after the trade deadline passed. Jimmy Garoppolo's name has been thrown around but realistically, it's hard to find any quarterback who is still unsigned in mid-November to save the season.

Former Patriots quarterback Matt Cassel, who is 44 years old, had some jokes.

It might not be that much worse than Tommy DeVito, who was awful in relief of Maye. He had 81 yards on 24 attempts. The Patriots had 91 yards on 11 possessions after Maye's injury. It was unwatchable football. And that's likely to be the case for the rest of the season.

Panic meter: Hey Patriots fans, at least the Celtics and Bruins are good.

Aidan Hutchinson was excited for a third-down sack on Drake Maye, so he jumped up and the landing might have ruined the Lions' Super Bowl hopes.

Hutchinson tore his ACL on the celebration, a brutal injury for Hutchinson and Detroit as well. Detroit was already without standout linebacker Jack Campbell. Hutchinson's knee injury removes the two best players from a defense that wasn't very good with them.

The Lions are 7-2 and Detroit fans will point to 2024, when the team went 15-2 after Hutchinson broke his leg. Perhaps they can get on a roll while being shorthanded. But realistically and unfortunately, the ceiling for the Lions was lowered with the injury.

Panic meter: The Lions shouldn't fall apart, but that celebration was a turning point in their season.

Everyone will have issues with the Vikings' blitz-happy defense this season. The Packers certainly have. They allowed eight sacks on Sunday and 12 in two meetings against the Vikings. While that's certainly too many, Green Bay has allowed only 13 sacks in their other eight games.

The problem is that the next opponent, after the bye, is Myles Garrett, Aaron Donald and the Rams. There's a lot of work to be done before that game the day before Thanksgiving.

The Packers lost 30-10 to the Vikings and they obviously haven't matched up well with Minnesota this season. But Green Bay was playing very well before Sunday's game and are still in fine shape in the NFC wild-card race.

Panic meter: As long as Brian Flores isn't on the other sideline, Green Bay should be fine.

The Texans' season can be viewed in an optimistic way, if you wish. Five of their losses this season have come on a drive by the opponent in the final minutes of regulation or in overtime. Close games like that should be close to 50/50 propositions, so with just a little more luck Houston would be right in the playoff picture.

But maybe it's not just luck. The Texans' defense, which was supposed to be good this season, has failed repeatedly when asked to close out a game. And regardless of how the Texans got to 3-6, that's where they are. And they have lost a key tiebreaker to the Browns, who are currently the No. 7 seed in the AFC.

Houston's playoff odds are 34.4% via DVOA, which seems high for a team three games under .500. That's because the AFC's wild-card race isn't very good. But, maybe the Texans aren't very good either.

Panic meter: The talent is still there, but the season is slipping away fast with bad losses.

#2725. Jerry Jones on Christian Parker's status: We're talking about that

Cowboys defensive coordinator Christian Parker failed to get a vote of confidence from head coach Brian Schottenheimer on Monday and he didn’t get one from Cowboys owner Jerry Jones on Tuesday either.

Parker’s defense has underperformed all season and they hit another low point during Sunday’s 46-40 loss to the 49ers. The Cowboys gave up 584 yards over the course of the game and the lowlights of the unit’s performance included three touchdowns that covered at least 66 yards.

Schottenheimer said on Monday that the Cowboys were “still in the process of going through all the film and evaluation of where we stand” when asked if there would be a change involving Parker. During an appearance on 105.3 The Fan on Tuesday, Schottenheimer’s comments were brought up and Jones was asked if the team is considering any changes involving Parker’s role or responsibilities heading into this week’s game against the Titans.

“We’re talking about that. I’m not going to talk about it here, but we are talking about those things,” Jones said.

Jones gave a similar answer to a question about simplifying the defense, but having these conversations 10 weeks into the season means that there’s an acknowledgement that the status quo isn’t working. It also means that anything other than a significant shift to the team’s defensive operations is unlikely to result in the kind of change the Cowboys need to salvage their season in the final seven games.

#2724. NBA panic meter: Which teams should worry after first month of season?

The NBA season is long. Too long, according to some critics.

One month of basketball, particularly the first month, is rarely enough to make definitive conclusions about a team's outlook. But it is enough time to form first impressions, identify flaws that loom as bigger problems and spot the teams in danger of not meeting expectations. It's enough time for a fan base to start panicking.

The first month of the 2026-27 NBA campaign has been especially challenging for several teams with playoff aspirations, with injuries and underwhelming performances already threatening to derail some of those hopes. At the same time, the defending champion New York Knicks, Miami Heat and Cleveland Cavaliers are among a handful of contenders stumbling out of the gate to the point that it's beginning to cause alarm.

So what's actually worthy of pushing the panic button and what's simply part of the natural ebb and flow of an 82-game season? Here's a breakdown of the NBA's panic meter after nearly a month of the 2026-27 regular season.

LEBRON JAMES: Climbs to third on NBA all-time assists list, passing Jason Kidd

The season is going off the rails quickly in Utah, with the Jazz mired in a 1-13 start and already dealing with injuries to several key players. Making matters worse, former cornerstone center Walker Kessler is flourishing with the Los Angeles Lakers after Utah opted to trade him this offseason rather than commit to a lucrative long-term extension. This was supposed to be the year the Jazz took a meaningful step forward and reentered the Western Conference playoff picture. Instead, they're already staring at a significant hole in the standings, and the front office is facing uncomfortable questions about whether its long rebuild is any closer to producing a contender.

Denver had won four of their previous five games entering Nov. 16, but their 7-4 start deserves closer scrutiny. The Nuggets' seven victories have come against opponents with a combined .383 winning percentage, while each of their four losses were by double digits against teams currently ahead of them in the Western Conference standings. Nikola Jokić has remained productive, but he hasn't looked quite as dominant as he did during his MVP peak, with a sharp decline in his 3-point shooting allowing defenses to guard him differently since his return from last season's wrist injury. Considering Jamal Murray is playing at a high level, Denver's inability to compete with upper-tier opponents is an especially troubling sign for a team that made no major roster upgrades after a first-round playoff exit.

The Cavaliers beat the Dallas Mavericks on Nov. 14 to get back to .500, but the circumstances only amplified concerns about Cleveland's underwhelming start. With the usual starters unavailable because of travel disruptions caused by the nationwide FAA ground stop, the Cavaliers' reserves stepped in and reinforced how poorly the team's top players have performed this season. Cleveland's projected starting lineup of James Harden, Donovan Mitchell, Peyton Watson, Evan Mobley and Jarrett Allen owns a -12.4 NET rating in 99 minutes together as of Nov. 16. Harden's slow start is concerning given his age, while Watson's struggles as both a shooter and defender have already sparked questions about his four-year, $88 million contract. This team reached the Eastern Conference finals last season and seems farther from returning than expected.

The Raptors were one of last season's biggest surprises in the Eastern Conference then doubled down on their win-now ambitions by acquiring Kawhi Leonard to provide the late-game scoring they lacked. But Leonard hasn't played since Oct. 23 after the team announced he would undergo a treatment and rehabilitation program to address swelling in his left knee. Toronto has gone 3-8 since then, including a current four-game losing streak punctuated by several blown leads. While an MRI revealed no structural damage, Leonard's lengthy injury history makes it difficult to assume a quick return to form. More concerning is that the Raptors still can't prove they can compete with the East's best teams. They are 0-7 against opponents with better records this season after going 5-22 in those situations a year ago.

The defending NBA champions opened the season 0-3, with an offense that has sputtered at times. New York's most-used lineup from a season ago – Jalen Brunson, Miles McBride, Josh Hart, Mikal Bridges and Karl-Anthony Towns – is also being outscored by 10 points per 100 possessions more than last year. But the Knicks have already climbed back above .500 thanks to a recent four-game winning streak. Towns is averaging a double-double and leading the NBA in rebounds, while Brunson remains one of the league's premier offensive orchestrators and shot creators. More importantly, this core proved during last year's playoff run that it can elevate its play when the stakes are highest. A slow start is worth monitoring, but New York has time to round back into championship form as the season progresses.

Plenty of NBA observers were ready to hit the panic button when the Heat opened the Giannis Antetokounmpo era with four consecutive losses. Since then, Miami has gone 7-2 while developing a lineup that appears to maximize the talents of both Antetokounmpo and Bam Adebayo after those initial growing pains. As of Nov. 16, the group of Antetokounmpo, Adebayo, Klay Thompson, Pelle Larsson and Davion Mitchell owns a 23.3 NET rating. Only the starting lineups of the Spurs and Lakers have played as many minutes and performed better. The Heat's climb up the Eastern Conference standings may not be perfectly smooth, but there are already indications that some of the early issues that plagued them are being resolved.

Tyrese Haliburton is already back on the injury report with a hamstring issue, and Ivica Zubac's integration into Indiana's up-tempo system hasn't been entirely seamless. Four of the team's five starters have already missed games. The Pacers learned last season how thin their margin for error becomes without Haliburton on the floor, and this year's success will depend heavily on whether he can consistently resemble the player he was before suffering a torn Achilles in Game 7 of the 2025 NBA Finals. Haliburton looked sharp during his first four games back, but Indiana's outlook could change quickly if his latest hamstring issue becomes a recurring problem.

#2723. Five Richland County girls soccer players earn All-Ohio honors

COLUMBUS — Five Richland County girls soccer players earned the ultimate honor of being named All-Ohioans for the 2026 season, as announce by the Ohio Scholastic Soccer Coaches Association on Tuesday, Nov. 17.

BOYS ALL-OHIO: Six Richland County boys soccer players earn All-Ohio honors

In Division III, Ontario's Kaylei Chandler and Lexington's Morgan Harrod were named All-Ohioans. Chandler had eight goals and 10 assists while playing nearly every position on the field and Harrod was one of the best scorers in Richland County with 28 goals and 10 assists.

In Division IV, Clear Fork coach Brittany Bechtel was named the OSSCA Coach of the Year after leading the Colts to a 16-3-2 record and a Division IV district championship after losing four starters to season-ending injuries throughout the season.

Clear Fork's Maci Roberts, Mansfield Christian's Brooklyn Rider and Shelby's Braylee Sturts earned All-Ohio honors in Division IV. Roberts led the Colts with 21 goals and 19 assists while Rider had 18 goals and nine assists for the Flames and Sturts made 307 saves in goal for the Whippets.

#2722. Andy Reid on Emmett Johnson: My hat goes off to him

When running back Kenneth Walker suffered a groin injury late in the practice week, it thrust rookie Emmett Johnson into a starting role for the Week 10 matchup with the Falcons.

Johnson made the most of that opportunity.

The rookie finished the contest with 101 rushing yards on 22 carries and caught six passes for 70 yards with two touchdowns, helping power the 35-13 victory.

Head coach Andy Reid complimented Johnson on his performance in his Monday news conference.

“My hat goes off to him — I mean, he had, what, 171 yards or whatever it was of total offense,” Reid said, via transcript from the team. “That’s a lot of contribution to the group there. So, we felt comfortable with that, that he could do that. It’s another thing of actually putting the product out there and doing it, but he’s very capable. He’s got the right attitude. He’s picked things up well. He’s a smart kid.

“So, we tried to give him that 100 [rushing] yards at the end, which we normally wouldn’t do, but he was so close that I thought it was kind of a neat deal for him.”

Johnson took 22 carries on Sunday after entering the contest with 26 on the season — illustrating how he can handle an expanded workload.

That may be important moving forward, as Walker has been one of the top utilized backs in the league so far in 2026.

#2721. Los Angeles Chargers news: Bolts’ commitment to Omarion Hampton apparent in 1st win

The Chargers began the season with Omarion Hampton as their lead back, but the second-year runner lost two fumbles in as many games to start the campaign and that quickly led the Bolts to a three-headed committee for the next month-plus.

In recent weeks, Hampton's been garnering a larger share of the backfield touches, but Monday night's performance was much more of what we all expected to be the norm week-in and week-out. Hampton carried the football more than 20 times and finished with 105 yards and a score, his first 100-yard game since Week 4 of last year. He looked strong, locked-in, and overall more comfortable as the game went on the more he touched the football.

This success on the ground or Hampton also coincided with a shift in the run game, going away from zone and more into gap and power schemes. Per James Koh of 97.1 The Fan in Los Angeles, the Chargers ran zone 53 percent of the time from Weeks One through Nine, but that number dropped to just 38 percent against the Ravens.

The shift away from zone for Hampton has been discussed at length this season, noting his success in gap schemes dating back to his time at North Carolina. On one of his best runs of the night, the Bolts pulled left tackle Joe Alt across the formation to kick out a defender in the hole and Hampton cut right off his backside for a gain of 19 to begin the second half.

Overall the Chargers ran for 164 yards on 34 carries, aided by Justin Herbert's 51 yards off scrambles, to end the night averaging a strong 4.7 yards per tote.

Is this the new recipe moving forward for the Chargers? I wouldn't be shocked in the slightest, if so.

#2720. Celtics still struggling to develop chemistry with Mitchell Robinson

BOSTON — Mitchell Robinson and the Boston Celtics still haven't figured each other out, and the disconnect stood out on Monday night.

Robinson's latest opportunity came against the Orlando Magic, with starting center Neemias Queta sidelined for a rest day. Robinson checked in roughly six minutes into the contest but lasted less than five minutes before being replaced by Luka Garza.

"It's a bigs-by-committee," Celtics head coach Joe Mazzulla said after Boston's 115-105 defeat at TD Garden, its first home loss of the season.

It's been 14 games. Robinson has played in every single one. He's made five starts and logged more than 17 minutes per night. Still, the 28-year-old hasn't gotten on the same page with his new Boston teammates.

Keeping Robinson on the floor became increasingly difficult, especially on the second night of a back-to-back in which the Celtics shot just 25 percent in the opening quarter. Teammates struggled to connect with him on several lob attempts, while Robinson had trouble making enough of an impact to warrant an extended look off Boston's bench.

Even though the effort from Robinson was apparent, it wasn't translating.

BOSTON, MA – NOVEMBER 16: Mitchell Robinson #4 of the Boston Celtics and Tristan da Silva #23 of the Orlando Magic grabs the rebound during the game on November 16, 2026 at TD Garden in Boston, Massachusetts. NOTE TO USER: User expressly acknowledges and agrees that, by downloading and/or using this Photograph, user is consenting to the terms and conditions of the Getty Images License Agreement. Mandatory Copyright Notice: Copyright 2026 NBAE (Photo by Brian Babineau/NBAE via Getty Images) | NBAE via Getty Images

Boston's inability to connect with Robinson on clean lob passes predates Monday night. It hasn't mattered whether the pass comes from Jayson Tatum or Derrick White. The Celtics have yet to iron out the wrinkles in their alley-oop execution with their new center.

Mazzulla admits it's not all on Robinson, to be fair.

"We threw a couple over the backboard a couple of times, I think also," Mazzulla said. "So I would say start there. Keep it below the backboard. I think that's a little bit of a timing thing. The lob play is a 2-on-1 read. It's a risk-averse situation. There has to be a great timing to it. The defensive big does a great job of timing it up. So it's not a bang-bang play. It's not an easy 2-on-1 read. It's something that you want to try to take advantage of."

Robinson finished with four points, five rebounds, two assists and a steal against Orlando. He also missed two free throws and picked up five personal fouls.

Boston's bigs combined for just seven rebounds. Franz Wagner alone grabbed eight, while Orlando's Goga Bitadze finished with six off the bench.

Not having Queta against a Magic team that leans into its size took a toll on the Celtics. With Robinson unreliable to deploy, Boston turned to Luka Garza and rookie Chris Cenac Jr. to fill the void. Garza didn't grab a single rebound and took five of his six shot attempts from 3-point range, while Cenac showed (limited) flashes of promise, finding his way into Boston's offensive flow. He finished with four points and two rebounds in just over 12 minutes.

Tatum spoke Cenac's flashes into existence, claiming the 19-year-old is "really, really going to be good," following Sunday night's win over the Dallas Mavericks.

He offered the Houston Cougars product some praise in the locker room again, speaking to the team's belief in their 27th overall selection in this past summer's draft, which Mazzulla also touched on.

Hearing about Tatum's vision for Cenac's future, Mazzulla expanded.

"He's got a lot of work to do, but I like his attitude," Mazzulla said pregame. "I think since Day 1, since he came in the summer, he's been over open-minded to just working and getting better. That's all I care about, especially at this age and stage of where he's at, and I thought that before [Sunday night vs. Dallas] because of his work ethic and his mentality and how he approaches everyday."

Cenac's lobs got TD Garden roaring and helped him start assembling a strong first impression as a pro.

"Chris stepped up," Tatum said, "especially with Neemi out. When his number was called, [he] was active on defense. Got out running in transition. He's a lob threat."

Still, that couldn't prevent the Celtics from getting outclassed by the 6-7 Orlando.

The Magic put the Celtics' frontcourt to shame, outscoring them 54-32. Orlando's final play — a fast-break dunk from a wide-open Jalen Suggs — sent the entire team into a frenzy. The Magic celebrated their first win in Boston since Dec. 18, 2022, while snapping the Celtics' six-game winning streak in the process.

Robinson was a liability, but Mazzulla remains optimistic for what's ahead for No. 4 in the still-young regular season.

"He'll continue to get better and better," Mazzulla said. "That's really the only thing is just the process of growth and him continuing to play the role that we need him to."

#2719. Matt LaFleur confirms Packers plan to have Micah Parsons return Week 12 vs. Rams

It was widely reported that edge rusher Micah Parsons was targeting the Week 12 game against the Rams for his return from reconstructive knee surgery.

Now that is getting closer to reality, as the Packers will be on their bye in Week 11 before playing in Los Angeles next Wednesday night.

Head coach Matt LaFleur told reporters on Monday that his plan is for Parsons to play, but that’s still not guaranteed.

“Well, that’s my plan, but I think my plan’s been different than everybody else’s the whole time,” LaFleur said, via Rob Demovsky of ESPN. “There’s still a couple more hurdles for him to clear before we can definitively say he’s a go. He’s shown progress, but we’ll see where he is by the end of the week.”

The Packers opened Parsons’ 21-day practice window earlier this month, setting him up for his return. He has been a limited participant in practice for all three days in each of the last two weeks.

#2718. Green Bay Packers News: Packers recovering from a meltdown

You never want to overstate things if you don't have to, but it's hard to avoid hyperbolic language after a full collapse like we saw on Sunday.

The offensive line was never really together to begin with, but even in its decimated state, it was especially bad against the relentless Minnesota Vikings.

And even accounting for the offensive line weakness, the passing game was nothing to write home about. The Packers' dynamic duo of wide receivers was largely neutralized, and Jordan Love missed badly on a red zone throw, resulting in an interception.

Meanwhile, on defense, the Packers were largely solid — except on third downs. Two massive conversions on third and long helped propel the Vikings toward their eventual victory, and the Packers were more or less just passive observers.

But aside from that, things were…well, they were still bad. A win, no matter how ugly or undeserved, was the only worthwhile outcome in this game. When you're scratching and clawing for divisional progress, there are no moral victories, and the gap between 7-3 and 6-4 in the win/loss column is massive, especially with the Rams and Bills looming after the Packers' bye week.

It was a terrible, horrible, no good, very bad weekend for your Green Bay Packers, and there's a lot of work to be done to get back on the right track.

#2717. UNC Basketball starter Neo Avdalas suffers wrist injury

The North Carolina Tar Heels faced their first big test last Friday, hosting Georgia in the Dean Dome. It didn't go as planned.

Unfortunately for the Tar Heels, they suffered their first loss of the season as the Bulldogs used a strong second-half push to earn the win. Now, the Tar Heels received news that they lost more than the game.

Starting wing Neo Avdalas suffered a wrist injury and is expected to miss an extended period of time, per Inside Carolina. Avdalas suffered the injury in the loss to Georgia and has been sidelined ever since. He had 14 points, three assists and two rebounds in that game.

Avdalas has been a starter in each of UNC's first four games this year, averaging 9 points, 3.5 rebounds and 3.8 assists. The Greek native was the first player to commit to Michael Malone in the transfer portal back in April and then earned his starting spot.

After four years in Greece playing professionally, he joined Virginia Tech last season and averaged 12.1 points, 3.1 rebounds, and 4.6 assists.

With Avdalas out for some time now, the Tar Heels will have to fill his role in the starting lineup. Freshman Maximo Adams has played really well in four games so far and could be a candidate to earn more playing time for the Tar Heels.

North Carolina is set to host Marshall on Friday night.

#2716. Spurs winning streak driven by Victor Wembanyama, but he is far from alone

Earlier this month, Victor Wembanyama, still in his uniform, sat at the podium and seemed almost disappointed.

He had just made history, generating a stat line that had only been replicated one other time since 1974. His 52-point, 24-rebound, 8-block masterclass on Nov. 4 propelled the San Antonio Spurs past the Dallas Mavericks in a one-point win.

But Wembanyama had been dealing with a cold, and it was only that night when he felt like he had finally gotten his legs back.

"It should be more like this," Wembanyama said. "More often."

The Spurs are the hottest team in the NBA, winners of 12 consecutive games. They've climbed to the top of Western Conference and are tied with the Los Angeles Lakers for best record in the NBA. San Antonio ranks within the top three in offensive rating (119.2), defensive rating (106.6) and net rating (+12.6). And one season after falling short in the NBA Finals, the Spurs are looking like a dominant team, one that could be poised for a championship run.

How exactly is San Antonio doing it? Throughout it all, it has been Wembanyama carrying the Spurs.

On Monday, Nov. 16, Wembanyama earned Western Conference Player of the Week, his second such honor in the regular season's first month. He's averaging career highs in points (28.6), rebounds (11.6) and blocks (4.3). But more than just the numbers, it's the way Wembanyama is playing the game that is elevating San Antonio.

Wembanyama is either attacking the rim and using his height to get high-percentage looks, or he's using his range to stretch the floor with his 3-point shot. He ranks second in the NBA in points in the paint per game (15.3), trailing only Miami Heat forward Giannis Antetokounmpo (17.9). Yet, Wembanyama leads all players in dunks with 65, which is 19 more than Antetokounmpo, the next closest player.

Wembanyama is getting to his spots down low and either slamming the ball home on his own, or giving his teammates an indomitable lob threat. Put another way: Wembanyama is not settling.

When the Spurs struggled against the New York Knicks in the Finals, it was typically because they fell in love with the 3-point shot and settled for perimeter jumpers. When San Antonio is its best, it's deploying its speedy guards in transition to attack the paint.

And this is where San Antonio's trio of guards come into play. With the promotion of Dylan Harper into the starting lineup, the Spurs now have the fastest starting backcourt in the NBA. De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle and Harper can each sprint up the floor on the break. They can each slash to the rim, and they can each kick out passes to open teammates. They also protect the ball.

The Spurs lead the NBA in fewest turnovers, committing just 12.3 per game. That has increased the efficiency with which the team plays, and that should only get better. Free agent addition Tobias Harris (calf) is expected to be sidelined through November, but he'll provide a perimeter shooting presence once he returns.

But even without Harris, teams have had to calculate which defensive assignments to prioritize. In a 38-point drubbing of the Warriors Friday, Nov. 13, Golden State practically dared Castle to shoot 3s, instead focusing on Wembanyama, Fox and Harper. Castle dropped 32 points and laced 5-of-8 shots from beyond the arc.

"Seeing teams have to scramble and switch matchups and over-rotate – I think that's when we're at our best," Castle said after the game. "When we have the ball popping and have them in rotations and we're getting drives and wide-open shots."

Granted, this 12-game winning streak has not come against the stiffest competition, so San Antonio will need to show it can sustain this level against elite opponents, especially when they're at full health. Tuesday night's matchup against the Detroit Pistons and a three-game stretch (Portland Trail Blazers, Lakers, Celtics) later in November should be telling.

Another thing that's telling is that the winning streak came after San Antonio dropped its first two games of the season and began Oct. 24 with a win against the Mavericks. Despite that victory, coach Mitch Johnson made it clear that more was to be expected from this group, challenging them to "get back to their standard."

So far, that challenge has been accepted.

This article originally appeared on USA TODAY: Spurs winning streak driven by Victor Wembanyama, but he is far from alone

#2715. Vikes Views: Nincompoop of the Week: Week 10 – Vikings at Packers

The Minnesota Vikings dismantled the Green Bay Packers, 30-10. Even in a 20-point blowout at Lambeau Field, somebody still earns the crown. Let's take a look at who was a Nincompoop this week.

The Vikings defense is going to make a lot of offensive lines look bad. The Packers offensive line was also missing a few key players. However, there are no excuses for what happened on Sunday. There were plays where four or five Vikings were in the backfield almost immediately. Jordan Love had no chance on seemingly half of his dropbacks. The eight sacks allowed were the most surrendered by any team in the NFL this season.

One of the reasons the Vikings were able to generate so much pressure was the Packers' game plan. The Vikings defense is susceptible to the run game, yet Green Bay never seriously tested them. Maybe that's because the Packers have one of the worst rushing attacks in football. They had some success when they ran the ball, but they abandoned it way too early. When your quarterback is getting battered and you keep asking him to throw deep, the results shouldn't be surprising.

If it wasn't for his 24-yard reception, Felton would have contributed negative yards to the offense. Two pre-snap penalties from a receiver already struggling to find his rhythm didn't help matters. Tai has just 99 yards in 26 career games. Felton didn't do anything obviously wrong, he'll just need to do a lot more if he wants to remain a regular part of the offense.

An early missed field goal, numerous penalties, and a late fumble on special teams didn't help the Packers' chances on Sunday. They were sloppy from start to finish. On a day when the Vikings' special teams unit was nearly perfect, Green Bay's special teams played a major role in the team's downfall.

Not a name I ever expected to put on this list. Darrisaw allowed another two sacks on Sunday, bringing his total to a career-high eight sacks allowed this season. The Vikings finally have competent quarterback play again, and the last thing they need is their franchise left tackle allowing defenders free shots at Kirk Cousins. Darrisaw is struggling to regain his form after a few injury-plagued seasons.

My vote goes to Matt LaFleur. I'd probably go LaFleur, Pack OL, Darrisaw, Pack Special Teams, and Tai Felton if I had to rank them.

#2714. “Aaron Rodgers is not good enough’: Analyst has harsh message for Steelers quarterback

The Pittsburgh Steelers lost 38-18 to the Cincinnati Bengals on Sunday Night Football in a game that was even more lopsided than the final score would indicate. Bengals quarterback Joe Burrow completely carved through what had been a well-respected Steelers defense coming into the game, finishing with 409 yards passing for four touchdowns.

As poorly as Pittsburgh's defense played, things were hardly any better on offense. Veteran quarterback Aaron Rodgers ended the game with 143 yards and two touchdowns, but nearly all of his production, including both touchdown drives, came in the fourth quarter with the game well out of reach.

On his 4th and 1 podcast, former NFL MVP Cam Newton did not mince words about the sort of quarterback he believes Rodgers is at this point in his career.

"That was embarrassing, to say the least. The Bengals destroying the Steelers," Newton said. "I just feel like Aaron Rodgers is not good enough to carry a team anymore. And when you look at DK Metcalf, it's extremely disappointing. And just looking around the board, going especially versus a Tee Higgins, a Ja'Marr Chase, a Joe Burrow, it's like you pick your poison with those guys.

"The Steelers on the other hand, it's only but so much a defense can do. It's only but so much Aaron Rodgers can try to create. I'm watching this game in sheer disappointment. Not that it messed up my picks, because it did. They could have done whatever they wanted to do it."

Newton went on to point out just how poor a job the Steelers did with possessing the ball with Rodgers at the helm.

"You get out-possessioned as far as for the time of the possession because you cannot sustain drives anytime you're going against a high potent offense," Newton went on. "You see the Bengals come down, move the ball effectively, efficiently, and all of a sudden they get points. To combat that, they at least move the ball and get, and they stall out in the red zone. The Steelers go three-and-out. They get the ball again, and then they go down there and score. Now, it's 10 to nothing while y'all still over here saying, 'Oh man, it's too cold out here.'

"That's how you get ransacked, slapped upside your head, punched in your mouth, kicked in your gut. Just a poor display of football. I wouldn't have expected this from a bad team. This was for first place in the AFC North. So, it wasn't like it wasn't a meaningful game. This was an extremely meaningful game that both teams needed to be at their best. One team showed up while the other was still in the parking lot talking about, 'Hey man, where my keys at?'"

As harsh as Newton's assessment may be, it's almost impossible to argue that he's off the mark after Pittsburgh was outgained by over 250 yards to lose first place in the AFC North. At 6-3, the Steelers and Rodgers are still in the playoff picture, but Rodgers and co. will need to find answers fast to have nay hope of ending the Steel City's drought of playoff wins.

#2713. UEFA Nations League: Kane inspires England to famous victory as Tuchel's men end Spain's 43-game unbeaten run

Thomas Tuchel's England ended Spain's 43-game unbeaten run with a hard-fought 3-2 win at Metropolitano Stadium. FC Bayern's striker Harry Kane opened the scoring from the penalty-kick he won before the half-time break as the 33-year-old became the only player to net in all six League A group stage games of the UEFA Nations League.

England were 3-0 ahead by the hour mark after goals from Arsenal defenders Ezri Konsa and Ben White, but Eliott Anderson's decision that led to a red card and a penalty for Spain changed the complexion of the game.

Having won the penalty-kick that was converted by Mikel Oyarzabal, Lamine Yamal assisted substitute Nico Williams for the second goal with 15 minutes left to play. However, Bayer Leverkusen's Jarell Quansah and co. produced a defensive masterclass for the rest of the game to secure a top spot finish for England.

Spain's unbeaten run ended with their last game of 2026 as the world champions suffered their first defeat since March 2024. Luis de la Fuente's side had to settle for a second-placed finish, with England having a better goal difference after avenging their defeat at Wembley by the same scoreline.

In the other game from the group, Croatia beat Czechia 2-1 thanks to goals from former Bundesliga stars Ivan Perisic and Lovro Majer. Hoffenheim's attacker Adam Hložek scored the only goal for Czechia, who are relegated to League B after losing all six games in the group.

#2712. Where Oregon's defense, special teams rank after Week 11 win

Facing a subpar Michigan Wolverines offense on Saturday, the standard for the Oregon Ducks was high defensively. If the Wolverines, who were held scoreless for the first 58:30 against Michigan State the previous week, scored more than a touchdown it would likely be considered some sort of disappoitment.

Well, good thing that wasn't the case. The Ducks allowed just seven points in the 31-7 victory, a fluky 70-yard rushing touchdown in which Wolverines running back Savion Hiter broke multiple tackles on his way to the end zone. But other than that, the Oregon defense locked down Michigan, both in the passing game and on the ground.

Considered to have above-average special teams units, Oregon performed well in that aspect of the game as well. Punter Bailey Ettridge, who a day earlier had been added to the Ray Guy Award Watchlist, did his thing on five punts. However, kicker Gage Hurych missed short from 46 yards out and isn't as reliable as he seemed earlier in the year, though it didn't take away from a strong special teams outing overall.

#2711. Through 10 weeks, the AFC playoff field is basically set

The 2021 expansion of the regular season to 17 games has given the bad teams more time to separate from the good teams. Which makes it easier for the eventual playoff teams to declare themselves.

This year, the seven AFC playoff teams have essentially declared themselves after only 10 weeks of games.

The most important developments in that regard were these: (1) the Browns beating the Texans; (2) the Ravens losing to the Chargers; and (3) the Patriots losing badly to the Lions — and losing quarterback Drake Maye, possibly for the rest of the season.

Already, we know that the Bills, Jaguars, Bengals, Broncos, and Chiefs are essentially locks to win tickets to the postseason party. The Steelers, despite being outclassed by the Bengals, have enough winnable games remaining to land a wild-card berth.

The only spot up for grabs is the seven seed. And the Browns are now in the driver's seat.

Already, four AFC teams are dead in the water: the 2-8 Jets, the bill1-8 Dolphins, the 1-8 Titans, and the 1-8 Chargers. The 3-7 Colts barely have a pulse, and it won't last long. That's five of 16 teams, with a fork already stuck in a spot where forks aren't supposed to go. And the 3-6 Texans aren't far behind.

The remaining contenders for the last spot in the playoff tree are the 6-4 Browns, the 5-5 Patriots, the 4-6 Raiders, and the 4-6 Ravens. New England could be in the process of imploding. The Raiders are 1-6 over their last seven games, and they're now starting a rookie quarterback. The Ravens have lost five in a row.

As a result, there's currently not much drama as to who the seven AFC playoff teams will be.

The good news is that the top of the seven-team hierarchy remains undecided — and it could stay that way for a while. The Bills, Jaguars, and Bengals stand apart from the rest. The Broncos and Chiefs remain in the mix. (The Steelers seem to be destined for another one-and-done postseason appearance, with a road game looming to Buffalo, Jacksonville, Cincinnati, or Denver/Kansas City.)

The bad news, and the inescapable reality, is that the have-nots have become crystal clear. With eight weeks of the season left.

For a league that loves parity, there isn't much of it when it comes to the line separating the playoff teams from the wait-til-next-year franchises.
